# Binding Description: Volume Q. 7

The volume is bound in a deep blue leather, featuring intricate **gold-tooled** <term>gold-tooled: a decorative technique where gold leaf is pressed into the leather using heated metal stamps</term> ornamentation. The craftsmanship reflects a high degree of artistry, characteristic of formal library bindings from the period.

The decorative scheme consists of:
*   A double-rule outer border.
*   A wide, elegant band composed of repeating floral and scroll motifs.
*   Four corner compartments, each housing a large, stylized **fleur-de-lis** <note>The fleur-de-lis is a stylized lily often associated with nobility or heraldry</note>.
*   An inner **dentelle** <term>dentelle: a lace-like decorative pattern pointed toward the center, resembling delicate lacework</term> frame impressed into the leather.

The central panel remains unadorned, highlighting the natural grain of the dark blue leather.

<margin>Q. 7</margin> <note>This alphanumeric code is a shelfmark, used by librarians to organize and locate the book within a specific collection.</note>

# Number in Catalogue XVI.

<note>The following text is written on a separate sheet of paper pasted onto the flyleaf; the right edge of this paper is torn and irregular, though the text remains legible.</note>

<insert>
This most singular work is the production of Masha'allah <note>original: "Messahalah"; a prominent 8th-century Persian Jewish astrologer and astronomer who wrote in Arabic</note>, an Arabian Astrologer. It was translated for the Dauphin <note>The title given to the heir apparent to the French throne</note> who was brought into England a Prisoner with his father John, King of France, by our Edward the Black Prince <note>Referring to the Battle of Poitiers in 1356 during the Hundred Years' War</note>. In all probability, the Dauphin brought the book with him, the translation having undoubtedly been made for him as appears by the red ink matter at the top of the opposite page. The Translator's name is on the last leaf. The date of the manuscript which appears on the Title to the second book is 1349.

Amongst the abundantly curious matter of this manuscript is the assertion that the earth (as since proved by Sir Isaac Newton) is flattened at the poles—a most remarkable fact to have been stated at a period when every Astrologer affirmed that the poles were elongated.

The Arms <note>Heraldic coat of arms</note> opposite are the Dauphin's to whom the Manuscript belonged.

He was afterwards King of France <note>Charles V, known as "the Wise," who was a noted patron of learning and astrology</note>.
</insert>

<summary>A descriptive note detailing the provenance of a 1349 manuscript by the astrologer Masha'allah, asserting it was owned by the French Dauphin while a prisoner in England. The author highlights a precocious scientific claim within the text regarding the Earth's shape at the poles.</summary>

<keywords>Masha'allah, astrology, King John II of France, Edward the Black Prince, Charles V of France, 1349, scientific history, polar flattening, provenance</keywords>

# Here begins the book that Sahl made concerning the judgments of astrology, translated from Latin into French by the command of Charles, eldest son of the King of France.

**I**n the name of our Lord, <note>The initial 'N' begins the phrase "In the name of..." (In nomine...)</note> Duke of Normandy and Dauphin, full of pity and mercy. Sahl ibn Bishr <note>original: "zehel de mesni"; Sahl ibn Bishr was a famous 9th-century Jewish astrologer and mathematician</note>, a man of the people of Israel, says: Know that there are 12 signs in the heavens. Six of them are masculine and six are feminine. Aries is a masculine sign and is among the signs of the day; Taurus is feminine and among the signs of the night. In like manner, the twelve signs follow one another, one masculine and the next feminine, until the end of the twelve signs. 

And of these twelve signs, there are six that mount and rise straight up over the horizon—that is to say, their ascendant or rising is quite straight—and six that rise obliquely, meaning they have an oblique and crooked ascent. Those that rise straight are from the beginning of the sign named Cancer until the end of the sign named Sagittarius. The reason for this is because the latitude of each is greater than its longitude, and it takes more than two equal hours <note>An "equal hour" is a fixed 60-minute hour, as opposed to "unequal hours" which varied by season</note>—meaning that each of these signs takes more than two equal hours to rise—and for this reason they are named "straight-ascending." 

Those that rise obliquely are from the beginning of Capricorn until the end of Gemini. This is because the latitude of each of them is less than the longitude, and it rises in less than two equal hours—meaning that each of these six takes less than two equal hours—and for this reason they are named "obliquely-ascending." 

And of these signs, some are called "movable" <note><term>movable</term>: now known as Cardinal signs, marking the start of the seasons</note>, namely: Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn. They are called movable because when the sun enters the beginning of these signs, the weather changes according to the season that follows. And four of these signs are "stable" and "fixed" <note><term>fixed</term>: signs that occur in the middle of a season</note>, namely: Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius. And they are named fixed because when the sun enters the co—

<meta>This page begins a detailed classification of the twelve signs of the zodiac, likely following a section on the cardinal or "movable" signs. at the beginning of them, the weather remains and is fixed in its state. That is to say, if it is cold, it will be cold; if it is the time of Spring <note>original: "ver"</note>, it will be Spring; and if it is Autumn, it will be the time of Autumn.

And four of these signs are common, and are called in ambiguous Latin <note>original: "ambigue recte"</note> "of two bodies" <note>These are the mutable signs, which occur at the end of a season and transition into the next.</note>; these are Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces. They are named common because when the sun is in the middle of them, the weather is mixed; the first part of them is hot and the other half cold, or the first half is cold and the other hot.

And some of these signs are four-footed, such as Aries, Taurus, Leo, Capricorn, and the end of Sagittarius.

And also, some are of the fire, that is to say: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius; and this is the first <term>triplicity</term> <note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element.</note>.

And the second triplicity is of the earth: these are Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn.

And the third triplicity is of the air, and signifies men, living things, and everything that is clear; these are Gemini, Libra, and Aquarius.

The fourth is of the water, and signifies water and everything that is in water: that is to say, Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.

And some of these signs are called dark and full of shadows: these are Libra and Capricorn. And there is a notable place which is called <term>combust</term> <note>From the Latin "combustus" (burnt). In astrology, this refers to a specific "burning" or debilitated area of the zodiac, usually the end of Libra and the start of Scorpio.</note>, and it is the end of Libra and the beginning of Scorpio.

And some of these signs have a faint voice: that is to say, Virgo, Capricorn, Aquarius, Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.

And some have a full voice: that is to say, Taurus, Aries, Gemini, Leo, Libra, and Sagittarius.

And among them are some who have no voice at all: that is to say, Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.

And some of these signs are for engendering sons or children who are mute, or "common"—meaning they are neither entirely fertile nor entirely <term>barren</term> <note>original: "brahengs"</note>: that is to say, Taurus, Gemini, and Aquarius.

And some are fertile with many children: that is to say, Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces.

And some of them are barren: that is to say, Aries, Leo, Virgo, Libra, Sagittarius, and Capricorn.

And some of these signs signify mountains and high places...

¶ There are 3 signs of fire which signify fire and everything that belongs to it or is made by it, and they signify noble men. The signs of the seed <note>Earth signs</note> signify the earth and all the containment of the earth and everything made from it. The signs of man <note>Air signs, often associated with human intellect</note> signify the air and everything that is raised from the earth. ¶ And the signs of water signify moist places and every work that is made of moisture. ¶ And the signs that generate many children signify assemblies of men. ¶ Therefore, the <term>triplicity</term> <note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element</note> of Aries is hot and dry and has the nature of <term>red choler</term> <note>The humor associated with fire, linked to a quick-tempered or ambitious personality</note>, and its part of the world is the East. Of this triplicity, the lords are the Sun by day and Jupiter by night, and Saturn is their participant both by day and by night.

<margin>2</margin>
¶ The triplicity of Taurus is cold and dry and has the nature and complexion of <term>black choler</term> <note>Also known as melancholy; the humor associated with earth</note>, and its part of the world is the South. Its lords are Venus by day and the Moon by night, and Mars is a participant with them both by day and by night.

<margin>3</margin>
¶ The triplicity of Gemini is hot and moist and has the nature and complexion of the <term>sanguine</term> <note>The humor associated with air, linked to a social and active personality</note>, and its part of the world is the West. The lords of this triplicity are Saturn by day and Mars by night, and Jupiter is a participant with them both by day and by night.

<margin>4</margin>
¶ The triplicity of Cancer is cold and moist and has the nature of <term>phlegm</term> <note>The humor associated with water, linked to a calm or stoic personality</note>, and its part of the world is the North. The lords of this triplicity are Venus by day and Mars by night, and the Moon is a participant with them both by day and by night.

Know that we have divided for each interrogation <note>A term used in horary astrology where a practitioner answers a specific question based on the stars</note>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological judgment, specifically focusing on the definitions and significations of the twelve houses in a chart.</meta>

and amended. And the thing for which one asks the meaning, by which it is signified—whatever thing it may be, whether of good or of evil—which has occurred in this <term>interrogation<note>A specific question posed to an astrologer to be answered by casting a chart for the moment the question is asked.</note></term> by the methods that the sages explained regarding the meaning of interrogations. And of things according to the substances of the twelve signs and the natures of the seven planets and the places from which things are taken, just as the planets have their own natures and meanings, as we shall arrange above by the pleasure of God.

### The First House

The first house is the entire beginning place where one begins the quest, in the place of the interrogation or of the birth or of the beginning of any work. And it signifies life and death, for it is the <term>ascendant<note>The degree of the zodiac rising over the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</note></term> of the life of man. And it goes before life when he issues from the womb of the mother. (And this line is ascending from the lowest part of the earth to the highest; it goes from darkness to light. And likewise, the rising from the darkness of the womb to the light of this world, and from the narrowness of the womb to the largeness of the air. And he who makes the request shall put his interrogation from the secret of his heart <insert>into light</insert> and it shall be the manifestation of what was previously hidden.) Therefore, it signifies the body and life and every beginning and movement of the body, and whatever thing shall be held from any ascendant; and the beginning of every healthy thing or the outcome, and every movable or rational thing, and the beginnings of all things.

### The Second House

The second house follows the ascendant and does not <term>regard<note>In astrology, "regarding" or "beholding" refers to the geometric aspects or lines of sight between different parts of the zodiac.</note></term> the ascending line; and it signifies the gathering of substance <note>Refers to personal wealth, possessions, and material resources.</note> and begins from life, and the following state, and the acceptance of promises and proximities or friendships.

### The Third House

The third house is not entirely removed from the ascendant. And it signifies one's siblings and kindred, one's neighbors and relatives, their well-being, and such similar things. Also faith and religion, contention in letters, epistles, legacies, pilgrimages, and dreams.

<summary>This text defines the astrological significations of the first three houses, linking the First House to birth and beginnings, the Second to wealth, and the Third to siblings and travel.</summary>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the catalog of the twelve astrological houses and their significances, picking up from the description of the first and second houses on the previous page. The text moves from the "bottom" of the chart (the Fourth House) through the western side toward the heavens.</meta>

### The Fourth House

The fourth house is called the <term>Angle of the Earth<note>In astrology, this is the Imum Coeli (IC), the lowest point of the chart, representing the midnight sun, one's roots, and the private life.</note></term> and signifies fathers and their status, birth, the root, decay, and prisons; cities and fields; towns and cities and every building; every thing hidden and moved, or hidden treasures; and death and that which follows after death—that is, what happens to the dead regarding their burial, such as whether the dead shall be buried or burned, or whether he shall be placed on a <term>bier<note>original: "chalyet" - a frame or stand on which a corpse or coffin is placed.</note></term>, or whether he shall be thrown into some pit, and such things that will happen to the dead. And it signifies the end of things, and houses, and lands—that is to say, inheritances and everything that is moved under the earth.

### The Fifth House

The fifth house <term>follows<note>A "succedent" house; it follows an angular house in the daily rotation of the sky.</note></term> the Angle of the Earth and is a place of love; for it belongs to the same <term>triplicity<note>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (fire, earth, air, or water).</note></term> and degree as the <term>ascendant<note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon.</note></term>. It signifies children and everything in which one has trust; messengers and gifts; honors and petitions; many friendships and certain cities and the festivities of their citizens, and fruits and inheritance.

### The Sixth House

The sixth house <term>falls away<note>A "cadent" house, which was traditionally considered a weak or "unfortunate" placement because it does not "aspect" or "see" the first house.</note></term> from the ascendant, nor does it look upon it at all. It is a malignant place and signifies illnesses, both curable and incurable; the timing of illnesses; male and female servants; <term>rustics<note>Peasants or those who work the land.</note></term>; and movements from one place to another.

### The Seventh House

The seventh house is the <term>Angle of the West<note>The descendant, the point where the sun sets, representing the "other" in one's life.</note></term> and signifies weddings and what belongs to them; and battles, contentions, and desires; and purchases that are made between two persons; pilgrimages; and the closeness of things between them. This line is adverse to the ascendant, and every planet that shall be in it is opposite to the ascendant.

### The Eighth House

The eighth house follows the Angle of the West and signifies death, infection, and deadly poisons; and fear, and everything regarding the inheritance of the dead; and everything one might possess; and battles, and certain <note>original: "heriaus" - possibly heirs or heralds; contextually, "heirs" is more likely given the mention of inheritance.</note> heirs, and adulteries, and impious people; and things that are held in trust, in guardianship, and baseness, and sorrow, and schemes.

### The Ninth House

The ninth house falls away from the ascendant and signifies pilgrimages and travel, and the power of the gods, and of all

<meta>This page continues the catalog of the twelve astrological houses. While the previous page concluded with the beginning of the Sixth House, this text picks up at the end of the Ninth House (the house of religion and philosophy), suggesting a gap in the source material for the Seventh and Eighth Houses. It then describes the final houses of the zodiac and explains the three primary classifications of the houses: Angular, Succedent, and Cadent.</meta>

...the houses of religion and philosophy and the science of all things. The wisdom of the stars and the presence of all things; divinations; epistles and <term>legends<note>original: "legns"</note></term>; and rumors; dreams themselves; and wisdom; and the things of the age to come; the presence of future things; holiness and religion; and all things past and present; and it seems that he [the planet or person] is a guest of honor and of great work.

### The 10th House
The 10th house is called the <term>Angle of the Sky<note>Also known as the Medium Coeli or Midheaven, representing the highest point in the sky at the moment of birth.</note></term> and signifies the king, or the exaltation of kings, and the empress; glory and memory; or the voice of praise, the masters, and wealth stolen and taken away; judges, princes, and <term>prelates<note>original: "p'las" - high-ranking church officials.</note></term>, and the status of masters.

### The 11th House
The 11th house <term>follows<note>A "succedent" house, which follows an angular house in the sky's rotation.</note></term> the Angle of the Sky; it signifies friends, trust, and fortune; the wealth of kings, their rents or their <term>tributes<note>original: "treuages" - taxes or dues paid to a lord.</note></term>; their knights and their infantry; the man who succeeds the king or the first prince; and it signifies praise and sons.

### The 12th House
The 12th house is <term>falling away<note>A "cadent" house, traditionally considered a weak or difficult placement.</note></term> from the <term>ascendant<note>The first house or the sign rising on the eastern horizon.</note></term>, nor does it <term>behold<note>The 12th house is "in aversion" to the 1st house, meaning they do not form a mathematical "aspect" or connection.</note></term> it; it signifies enemies, <term>deceptions<note>original: "d'aloius" - likely meaning bad faith or lack of integrity.</note></term>, lamentations, hauntings, misfortunes, sorrows, enemies, and travails; malevolence, pilgrimages, labors, and malice, poisons, and <term>letters<note>original: "lestres" - possibly meaning secret correspondences or perhaps a misspelling of "bestes" (beasts).</note></term>.

And the most worthy of all these places, and the most laudable, primarily in their chief power and strength, are distinguished thus:

The circle is divided into 12 signs, of which 4 are named **Angles**: namely, the ascending sign, the 4th, the 7th, and the 10th. These signs are like the corners of the sky, which signify everything that is present in things—what is happening now—and the presence of things, and the power in all things.

And from these, four are called **Succedent**, or followers of the angles, which come after them: namely, the 2nd, the 5th, the 8th, and the 11th. These signify everything that is to come, and that which is <term>stable<note>original: "ferment" - meaning things that are becoming fixed or established.</note></term>.

The other four are called **Cadent**, falling away from the angles, which draw apart and fall from the angles: they are the 3rd, the 6th, the 9th, and the 12th. These signify everything that is past; this is clear by the will [of God].

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical ranking of the astrological houses based on their "strength" or influence on a person's life. The previous page ended while describing the 12th house as a place of hidden enemies; this page elaborates on why certain houses are considered powerful (like the Ascendant and the Midheaven) and why others are considered "weak" or "unfortunate" (like the 8th, 6th, and 12th) based on whether they can "see" the Ascendant.</meta>

...God. The strongest place in the sky is the <term>Ascendant<note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth, representing the self and the physical body.</note></term>, and this line is the most worthy of all the signs. The planet that is there is the strongest of all planets, especially if that planet is in its own <term>house<note>The sign it naturally rules.</note></term> or in its <term>exaltation<note>A sign where a planet's influence is particularly harmonious or elevated.</note></term> or <term>Almuten<note>original: "alphate" - from Arabic 'al-mubtazz', meaning the planet that holds the most dignities or 'victory' over a specific degree.</note></term> or however one designates it.

And following this in strength is the <term>Angle of the Sky<note>The Midheaven or 10th house, representing career and public reputation.</note></term>. And after that, the <term>Western Angle<note>The 7th house, where the sun sets, representing partnerships.</note></term>, which is the 10th <note>The text says '10th' here, but the Western Angle is traditionally the 7th; this may be a scribal error or a specific counting method.</note> from the Ascendant. 

And next follows the <term>Earth<note>original: "la tre" (la terre). This refers to the Imum Coeli or 4th house, the lowest point in the chart.</note></term>, which is the 5th <note>The text says '.v.e' (5th), though 'the Earth' is traditionally the 4th house.</note> from the Ascendant. And following this house in strength are the 11th house from the Ascendant, then the 9th house, and then the 5th. Both the 5th and the 9th are places that are praiseworthy and strong. The first place is better than the second, the second better than the third, and so on.

And after these, one chooses those signs known to be good: the 3rd sign from the Ascendant because it is the <term>place of joy<note>A traditional concept where each planet 'rejoices' in a specific house; the Moon's joy is the 3rd house.</note></term> of the Moon; and then the 2nd place from the Ascendant because it is the one that rises next.

And misfortune is very great in the 8th sign from the Ascendant because it is the <term>House of Death<note>Associated with mortality, inheritance, and the resources of others.</note></term>, and it does not "look at" the Ascendant <note>In traditional astrology, houses that do not form a geometric aspect to the 1st house are said to be 'in aversion,' making them difficult or 'blind'.</note>. 

The other remaining signs of the sky—the 6th and the 12th—are the worst of all places, for they will be of no profit. For the 6th sign from the Ascendant is the place of various illnesses; it <term>falls away<note>A 'cadent' house, which is traditionally considered weak because it is moving away from the powerful 'angles'.</note></term> from the Ascendant and does not "look at" it; it is the place of the <term>Joy of Mars<note>Mars, the planet of conflict, 'rejoices' in the 6th house of illness and toil.</note></term>.

And the 12th from the Ascendant is the place of enemies, of labor, and of sadness, and it "falls away" from the Ascendant and does not "look at" it; it is the place of the <term>Joy of Saturn<note>Saturn, associated with restriction and melancholy, 'rejoices' in the 12th house of hidden troubles.</note></term>, for Saturn rejoices in lamentation, complaint, and tribulation.

**U**ne such <term>conjunction<note>original: "qunction" — a variant of the astrological term for when two planets meet.</note></term> is when the planet which is light and swift seeks the conjunction of another planet that is slow and heavy; and let the lighter planet be at fewer degrees than the slower one, and for as long as it moves toward it until it is joined to it, it is in such a state by regard. <term>amithadul<note>The French scribe’s attempt at the Arabic *al-muttasil*, meaning "the applying" or "the reaching" planet.</note></term> this means "conglutinative" <note>joining together as if with glue</note>. ### Separation or Departure
And concerning the **separation** or departure of the planets: it occurs when the swifter planet passes the heavier one, so that it no longer has any degrees [of contact], neither in <term>regard<note>original: "regard" — the aspect or geometric relationship between signs.</note></term> nor in <term>conjunction<note>When two planets occupy the same degree in the zodiac.</note></term>. For "regard" is measured from sign to sign, and "conjunction" is said to be from degree to degree. This judgment is from <term>Masha'allah<note>original: "messehallah" — a famous 8th-century Persian Jewish astrologer whose works were foundational to medieval European astrology.</note></term>; it means "what God wills to be master" <note>The author provides a French interpretation of the Arabic name Masha'allah ("What God Wills").</note>.

### Transfer of Light
The **transfer of light** <note>original: "Translation de lumiere" — a technique where a fast-moving planet acts as a bridge between two slower planets that cannot "see" each other.</note> from planet to planet occurs when the swifter planet is separated from a heavier one and is [then] joined to another. Therefore, it is as if it had joined them together; it carries the nature of the first planet to the other to which it is joined. 
An example of this: suppose **Virgo** was the <term>ascendant<note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment a question is asked.</note></term> and a question was asked regarding a **marriage**. And the **Moon** was at 10 degrees of **Gemini**, **Mars** at the 9th degree of **Leo**, and **Jupiter** at the 12th degree of **Pisces**. In this case, Mars—who was the lord of the ascendant and the <term>significator<note>The planet designated to represent the person asking the question.</note></term> of the inquiry—did not "see" Jupiter, who is the lord of the <term>house of marriage<note>The 7th house in an astrological chart, which governs partnerships and contracts.</note></term>, for Jupiter was in the 8th sign from him <note>Signs that are in an "inconjunct" relationship (five signs apart) were traditionally said not to be able to form an aspect.</note>. Therefore, I looked to the Moon, which I found at the 10th degree of Gemini, separated from Mars and joined to Jupiter. Thus, she carried the light between these two and signified the outcome of the matter—that is, the acquisition of the wife through the hands of a **messenger** <note>original: "legat" — an intermediary or legate.</note> who goes from one to the other.

### Collection of Light
**Collection of light** <note>original: "Conjunction de lumiere" — usually known in astrology as *recollectio luminis*, where two planets both move toward a third, heavier planet.</note> is when the lord of the ascendant and the lord of the thing sought are both joined to a single heavier planet alone, which joins their force and their [strength...]

For example: a question <note>original: "interogacion" — in horary astrology, this refers to a specific question asked of the stars.</note> was asked by a king to know whether he would acquire a kingdom or not. The **ascendant** <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question, representing the person asking.</note> was the sign of **Libra**; therefore **Venus** was the lady [ruler] and signifier of the question, and she was at 16 degrees of **Aries**.

And the **Moon** was the lady of the royal house <note>The 10th house of an astrological chart, which governs kingdoms, power, and high office.</note> which signified the Kingdom, and she was at 12 degrees of **Taurus**; the two did not "see" each other.

And **Jupiter** was at 14 degrees of **Cancer** and in the "angle of heaven"—that is, in the royal house—and both the Moon and Venus were joined to him. Therefore, Jupiter joined the light—that is to say, the king and all those factors—at the place of the thing sought, which is the place of the kingdom. This signified the acquisition of the kingdom through the hands of some duke or bishop, or through the hands of some beloved soul to whom the two planets willingly give their assent.

### Prohibition
**Mana** <note>From the Arabic *man'*, meaning "prevention" or "prohibition."</note>, which is prohibition or defense, is performed in three ways. The first of these is called the **removal of light** <note>original: "ablaison de lumiere"</note>. This occurs when, between the lord of the ascendant and the lord of the thing sought, there is another planet with fewer degrees than either of them, and its **conjunction** <note>When two planets reach the same degree.</note> is such that it joins with the lord of the matter.

For example: suppose **Virgo** was the ascendant and a question was asked regarding a marriage. **Mercury**, the lord of the ascendant and signifier of the questioner, is at 10 degrees of **Cancer**. **Jupiter** was the lord of the 7th house—which signifies the spouse—and was at 14 degrees of **Pisces**. **Mars** was at 11 degrees of **Aries**. 

Therefore, Mars "removed the light" from Mercury to Jupiter. Furthermore, Mars was in the 8th sign from the ascendant—which is the sign of the "substance of the woman" <note>The 8th house represents the partner's financial resources or dowry.</note>. Thus, it signified that the destruction of this matter [the marriage] would be caused by the loss of the **dowry**.

The second way is when the swift planet and the heavier planet are both in the same sign.

The third occurs between them in

¶ For example: let the **ascendant** <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question.</note> be **Cancer** and the inquiry be made about **marriage**. ¶ The third manner is when the lighter planet is joined to the heavier planet in one sign, and there is another planet joined by **aspect** <note>original: "regart" — an astrological "look" or angle between planets in different signs.</note> to that heavier one, which is [positioned] before the lighter one in degrees—that is, having fewer degrees.

¶ Therefore, the light planet that is in the same sign with the heavy one forbids the conjunction of the other which "looks" [aspects] it; and when it has passed the conjunction, it will be void of it. This manner similarly destroys the causes or the matters, and ruins them just as the others previously mentioned.

¶ For example: let **Cancer** be the ascendant and the request be about marriage. Let **Mars** be at 10 degrees of **Taurus** and **Saturn** be at 23 degrees of that same sign. Since **Mars** was within the degrees of the **Moon**—at fewer degrees—it performed a **removal** <note>original: "ablasion"</note> of the aspect between the **Moon** and **Saturn** and prohibited their conjunction. For **Mars** was joined to **Saturn** in one sign, and this conjunction is stronger than a conjunction by aspect; for an aspect does not destroy a conjunction, but a conjunction destroys an aspect. An aspect does not perform a removal of another aspect, but rather gives assent to the matter, whereas a conjunction takes away the aspect.

¶ And sometimes one planet is joined to another, but before <note>original: "ainçois"</note> it reaches it, it is joined to another [first], and when it is joined to that one, the [original] conjunction is destroyed.

...in its descent, or whether it has descended while voiding its disposition. Whenever that voiding planet performs the action, it signifies that the matter will come to pass for her.

The explanation of **void of course** <term>void of course</term> <note>A term used when a planet, particularly the Moon, completes all its aspects with other planets before leaving its current zodiac sign. It is usually considered a time when "nothing will come" of a situation.</note> is when the Moon or a planet is empty. The planet is then like a wanderer, and therefore it is called "void of course." This occurs when it is not joined to any planet, and no planet is joined to it.

The explanation of **return** <term>return (restoration)</term> <note>Also known as 'restoration of light,' this occurs when a planet is prevented from completing an aspect, but the influence is 'returned' or restored by another planet's movement.</note> is when the planet or the Moon is joined to a **retrograde** <term>retrograde</term> <note>When a planet appears to move backward in the sky from our perspective on Earth; it often signifies delays or reversals.</note> planet or is **under the sun's rays** <term>under the sun's rays</term> <note>A planet is "combust" or weakened when it is too close to the sun, as its own light and influence are overwhelmed by the sun's brightness.</note>. In this case, it returns what it has received from that planet, and the cause <note>The 'cause' refers to the subject or goal of the astrological question.</note> is attained.

And the other manner of **return** and of **destitution** <term>destitution</term> <note>A condition where the intended outcome of a planetary relationship is abandoned or fails to materialize.</note> is when the voiding planet—that is to say, the light planet <note>Fast-moving planets like the Moon or Mercury are called 'light' in medieval astrology.</note>—is joined to a heavy and **cadent** <term>cadent (falling)</term> <note>A 'cadent' planet is one located in a house that 'falls away' from the powerful angles of the chart, often indicating weakness or lack of effectiveness.</note> planet, and the light planet is falling from the **ascendant** <term>ascendant</term> <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question, representing the person asking.</note>. Such a planet returns what it has received from the other and destroys its disposition or arrangement. This signifies that this **interrogation** <term>interrogation</term> <note>original: "interrogacion" — in horary astrology, this is the specific question posed to the astrologer.</note> shall have neither beginning nor end. 

For example: let the ascendant be such that the Moon is in **Sagittarius at the 5th degree**, falling from the ascendant, and let it be joined to Mars; and let Mars be in **Gemini at the 12th degree**, falling from the ascendant. This then signifies the destruction of the **seeking** <term>seeking (quereureument)</term> <note>original: "quereureument" — the object or purpose of the inquiry.</note> and the end of this interrogation. There is another manner of return where a light planet is joined in an **angle** <term>angle</term> <note>The 'angles' (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses) are the most powerful positions in an astrological chart.</note>, and is joined to a planet falling from the ascendant. In this case, the matter will have a beginning because the planet that is joined—which signifies the start of the thing—was in an angle. However, it will not have an end, because its receiver—which signifies the end of the thing—was falling. The one that is joined is light, and to it belongs the beginning of the matter; the receiver is the one that is heavy, and it is named the receiver of the disposition or arrangement.

The explanation of this matter: that the planet be joined to another of its house, or of its triplicity, or of its...

**E**xplanation of the besieged <note>original: "allegie"; from the Latin 'alligatio,' referring to a planet being 'bound' or 'besieged' between two difficult influences.</note> planet is that the planet be between two malefic ones, separated from one of them and joined to the other, without throwing its rays or without the projection of light of another planet between them; that is, that no other planet besides one of those two throws its rays between them. --- Page 28 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of planetary influences, shifting focus from the "joys" of the planets to the specific role of the Moon as a mediator and the general nature of malefic and benefic planets.</meta>

Her health <note>Referring to the Moon, as "elle" is feminine in French and the context describes the lunar cycle.</note> is the health of all things, and her destruction and diminishment is the diminishment of all things. And when she separates <note>original: "desuoye"; in astrology, this refers to a planet moving away from an aspect with another.</note>, it is because she commits her disposition to the one she approaches most closely and to whom she is joined; she unites her being with the planet upon which she casts her rays. And to those planets to which she is joined, she transforms her being into that of that planet. And that planet is called the **receiver of the disposition** <note>A technical term in astrology where one planet "hands over" its influence to another.</note> because it receives that which had been entrusted to it. Thus the Moon is the carrier of these planets and is called the **bringer of light** <note>original: "rapporte lumiere"; likely referring to "Translation of Light," where the Moon moves from one planet to another, connecting their energies.</note> from some of them to the others.

## The malefic planets signify destruction and evil
For the cause of their accidents and properties; this is because the excess of their power—either of cold or of heat—which enters into them, creates an obstacle. But if the planet is in the house of a malefic, or if she finds it in her **exaltation** <term>exaltation</term> <note>A specific sign in the zodiac where a planet is thought to be exceptionally strong or "dignified."</note>, or if it restrains its malice from her, or if the aspect <note>original: "regard"; how planets "look" at each other through specific angles.</note> from the malefics is a **trine** <note>original: "du tiers"; a 120-degree angle, considered harmonious.</note> or a **sextile** <note>original: ".6. regart"; a 60-degree angle, also considered favorable.</note> aspect, they are also kept temperate; for this aspect remains without any harm. And the **benefics** <note>original: "les fortunes"; referring to Jupiter and Venus, which bring good luck.</note> are of a different nature and of balanced temperament; that is, they are centered in heat and humidity. They always bring things to perfection, whether they have received a planet or not, but **reception** <note>When a planet is in a sign ruled by the planet it is aspecting.</note> in them is more profitable and better.

## There are two kinds of stars, which are good and bad.
Therefore, in whatever place you see the malefic planets, say that it is bad; and in whatever place you see the good ones, say that it is good.

## And a planet is not called "hindered" 
Until the malefic casts its rays upon its light, according to the distance that they cast from their **orbs** <note>original: "rondesses"; the circular area of influence surrounding a planet.</note>. And when it has passed the ruin of the malefics, it is called "regarding," and the evil can no longer hinder it. And when a malefic planet...

**A** **retrograde planet** <term>retrograde</term> <note>When a planet appears to move backward in the sky; traditionally viewed as a sign of weakness or reversal.</note> signifies disobedience and dissolution, as well as involution, retraction, and turning back, contradiction, impulse, re-contradiction, and sweetness or discord.

**T**he **malefic planets** <note>referring to Saturn and Mars</note> liquefy difficulty and bring pain and haste in their movement.

And it does similarly when it is in the **houses** <term>houses</term> <note>The zodiac signs ruled by specific planets.</note> of Saturn or Jupiter; and in the houses of the light <note>fast-moving</note> planets it [acts similarly].

**W**hen the **Moon** is joined to any planet <insert>hastily</insert>, it thus perfects its **conjunction** <term>conjunction</term> <note>When two planets reach the same degree and minute of the zodiac.</note>; this is when it is in the same minute with it. Look at what is to come from the investigation of the planet to which the Moon will be joined after that.

**W**hen a planet is at the last degree of a sign <note>original: "ligne"; here meaning a sign of the zodiac.</note>, its power has already left that sign, and the power will be in another sign. It is just like a man who has put his foot upon the threshold of the door and wishes to go out; and if the house falls down, it [the fall] hinders him [less]. And if the planet is at the 24th degree, the power of that planet will be in that same sign, for every planet has three degrees where its virtue is spread: that is, the degree in which it is, the one after, and the one before.

**A** planet sometimes seeks a conjunction, but it does not have it in its sign unless it is sent there by haste; and when such a planet enters into another sign, it is not joined to the other, and the matter <note>original: "cause"; the situation or question being asked of the stars.</note> is not perfected. And if it is joined to another and accompanied, and after that another joins with it, the matter will not be perfected, because it is mixed there with the light of another planet.

**T**he planet that wishes to join another planet in a sign, but cannot reach it in that sign, and reaches it when it is in another sign, the matter is then perfected.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for interpreting planetary movements in astrology, following the discussion of retrograde motion and planetary stations from page 12. ...if it is not already joined to another planet beforehand. Afterward, if it is joined to another by **aspect** <term>aspect</term> <note>original: "regart"; the geometric relationship or "look" between planets across the zodiac.</note>, it does not hinder it because of what I have already said. For the **conjunction** <term>conjunction</term> <note>original: "giunction"; when two planets occupy the same degree of the zodiac.</note> that is made by aspect does not nullify the conjunction that is made by body (physically) within a single sign. This physical conjunction nullifies the one made by aspect. An aspect does not take away another aspect, but it takes away the underlying cause; however, a physical conjunction takes away the aspect entirely.

When a **malefic planet** <note>original: "mauuaise planeite"; typically referring to Saturn or Mars, which were thought to bring challenges.</note> is **oriental** <term>oriental</term> <note>Rising in the east before the Sun.</note>—that is, when it rises in the morning in the east within its own **house** <term>house</term> <note>The zodiac sign it naturally rules.</note> or its **exaltation** <term>exaltation</term> <note>A specific sign where a planet’s influence is most powerful.</note>—and it is not joined to another malefic that might hinder it, then even a **benefic planet** <note>original: "fortūe"; usually Jupiter or Venus, associated with good luck.</note> that is **retrograde** <term>retrograde</term> <note>Appearing to move backward.</note> and hindered is better and more worthy than it.

When the malefics are the lords of the "causes" or the matters at hand, and the lord of the **ascendant** <term>ascendant</term> <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of the question.</note> is joined to them—or if the **Moon** is joined by a **square** <term>square</term> <note>original: "quart regart"; an aspect of 90 degrees, often considered stressful.</note> or by **opposition** <term>opposition</term> <note>An aspect of 180 degrees.</note>, or from the fourth sign, or from the tenth <note>original: ".d."; likely an abbreviation for "dixième" (tenth), referring to the 10th house or a square aspect.</note>—they bring about the cause, but they destroy it in the end. And if those that are joined are malefics, it will be better if they are "receiving the disposition"—that is, if those malefics are inclined toward goodness and are joined to the other planets.

When a malefic is in the ascendant in its own house or exaltation, its capacity for evil is restrained, unless it is retrograde while in the ascendant. If it is retrograde there, its evil is doubled, and its hardness and **harshness** <note>original: "ruiditōn"; roughness or severity of character.</note> are multiplied.

When a planet is in its proper **dignity** <note>original: "habit"; literally "dress" or "habit," referring to its state of power.</note> and the nature of its sign is suitable to it—for example, if **Saturn** is in its house or exaltation in a "cold" sign, or if **Mars** is, as I told you before, in a "hot" sign—that will be good. ¶ But if the planet is in a sign contrary to its nature, it will be bad, just like water and oil which do not mix nor can they be joined together. If it is in a sign similar to itself, it will mix just like water and milk.

When the benefics look upon (aspect) the malefics, they **lessen** <note>original: "apetisēt"; to diminish or make small.</note> the malefics' power to hinder. ¶ When the malefics look upon the benefics by square or opposition, they diminish the benefics' good fortune.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for planetary strength and debility, specifically addressing how "benefic" (fortunate) and "malefic" (unfortunate) planets change their behavior based on their position in the zodiac signs and their proximity to the Sun. **W**hen the **benefics** <note>original: "fortunes"; planets considered naturally lucky or helpful, like Jupiter and Venus.</note> are **cadent** <term>cadent</term> <note>original: "cheantes"; falling away from the powerful "angles" of the chart, which weakens their influence.</note> from the **ascendant** <term>ascendant</term> <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</note> or **retrograde** <term>retrograde</term> <note>Appearing to move backward across the sky.</note>, they will be hindered and act similarly to **malefics** <note>original: "mauuaises"; planets associated with challenges or hardship, like Saturn and Mars.</note>.

**W**hen the planets are **received** <note>original: "retenues"; a technical term for "reception," where a planet is hosted by another in a sign it rules, creating a supportive bond.</note> and are benefics, their essence will be stronger; and if they are malefics, their hindrance will be even stronger.

**W**hen the planets are in a **strange line** <note>original: "ligne estrange"; referring to being "peregrine," or having no special standing or "dignity" in that part of the zodiac.</note>—that is, when they are neither in their **houses** <term>house</term> <note>The sign a planet naturally rules.</note>, nor in their **exaltations** <term>exaltation</term> <note>A specific sign where a planet’s influence is most honored and powerful.</note>, or their **triplicity** <term>triplicity</term> <note>A group of three signs sharing the same element: fire, earth, air, or water.</note>—they increase the evil, and their hindrance is grown. And when they are in the lines where they have **witnesses** <note>original: "tesmoings"; referring to planets that provide support through their placement or aspects.</note>, they are held back from doing evil, but the hindrance is not removed entirely.

**W**hen the malefics are the lords of matters while in their own houses, or exaltations, or triplicities, or in their **bounds** <term>bounds</term> <note>original: "fines"; specific subdivisions of a zodiac sign ruled by different planets.</note>, or **angles** <term>angles</term> <note>The four most powerful houses in a chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th.</note>, and in the signs following the angles, their power will be just like the power of the benefics, and it stands as I have told you.

**W**hen the benefics are in a line where they have no witness, their good fortune and their benefit is diminished; and when they are in a line where they have a witness—that is, in their houses, exaltations, triplicities, or their bounds—their good fortune is increased, and the perfection of the matter is increased in goodness.

**W**hen the benefics and the malefics are in a bad place—that is, in any of the houses I have mentioned—or when they are **under the rays** <note>original: "soubz tous les rays"; being so close to the Sun that they are invisible in the sky.</note>, they signify a small and contemptible thing. The planets cannot signify good or evil because of the weakness that is in them; for when the planet is under the rays, **combust** <term>combust</term> <note>Specifically within 8.5 degrees of the Sun, thought to "burn up" the planet's power.</note>, or in opposition to the **Sun** <note>original: "soleil".</note>, it will be weak. In this place, there is no utility; there is no good in the good planets, nor any evil in the bad ones, for the benefics signify only a little good when they are under the rays. And similarly, when the malefics are under the rays, their hindrance will be less.

Therefore, consider how they look upon the positions of the planets—that is, their positions relative to the ascendant and the zodiac signs where they are located. For notwithstanding that a planet may be malefic, if it is in its own likeness, or in its light, or in its house, or exaltation, or in its triplicity, or in a good position relative to the ascendant, it signifies good.

From 5 degrees until it is within a half-degree <note>original: "v a iour"</note> of the sun, the planet is made as weak as it can possibly be.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions on the qualities of the zodiac signs and planetary movements. firmness, and being fixed, and the stability of matters about which a
request is made. ¶ And when they are in **common signs** <note>original: "signes comuns"; also known as mutable signs (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces), which represent transition and flexibility.</note>,
this signifies the appearance of a matter and repetitions,
applying that thing to itself or to some other matter. And when they are in **movable signs** <note>original: "signes mouvables"; also known as cardinal signs (Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn), which initiate action and change.</note>, it signifies
swiftness <note>original: "isnelete"; an Old French term for speed or agility.</note> and change and the mutation of matters, whether for good

that the matter is not singular, and things that cannot
be done, and all things that are repeated a second
time. And a **movable sign** signifies swiftness and the
mutation of one thing into another.

When the planet moves into **retrogradacion** <note>The apparent backward motion of a planet in the sky, usually interpreted as a time of retreat or complication.</note>—that is,
when it is in its **first station** <note>The point where a planet appears to stop moving forward before turning retrograde.</note>—this signifies the
dissolution of the cause and disobedience. And when it moves toward
**direction** <note>Forward movement in the zodiac.</note>—that is, when it is in the **second station** <note>The point where a planet appears to stop moving backward before resuming forward motion.</note>—this
signifies progress after the delay of a matter or hardship.
And every planet that is a **significator** <note>original: "signifianteur"; the specific planet that represents the person asking the question or the subject of the inquiry.</note> and wishes to
strengthen itself—that is, if it is in its second station—this
signifies the renewal of matters and their inspiration <note>original: "aspiron"; likely a variant of "aspiration" or "breathing life into."</note>, and strength and
direction. ¶ And if it is in the first station and intends to be
**retrograde**, this signifies the destruction of matters and delay.

Know that on the day the **moon** is in **confusion** <note>original: "confousion"; likely referring to the moon being "void of course" (making no further connections to other planets) or otherwise afflicted.</note>,
it releases <note>original: "relache"; meaning the matter loses its grip or fails to materialize.</note> all that which is asked about on that day.
Likewise, it is hindered if the **malefic** <note>original: "la mauvaise"; referring to a planet with a traditionally difficult influence, such as Saturn or Mars.</note> that hinders it is not **cadent** <note>original: "cheante"; falling away from the main angles of the chart, a position that weakens the planet's power to act.</note>
from the **ascendant** and weak, and if the moon then has no part
in the ascendant; for when the malefic hinders

[fear] and worry. And when she <note>The Moon.</note> is in the **angles** <note>The four most powerful houses in a horoscope: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses.</note> and in the **succedent houses** <note>original: "ensiuuātes"; the houses immediately following the angles (the 2nd, 5th, 8th, and 11th houses).</note> of the angles, she sends much benefit to the body.

If she is joined to the **fortunes** <note>The "benefic" or lucky planets, traditionally Jupiter and Venus.</note>, it signifies good profit; and if she is joined to **malefics** <note>original: "mauuaise"; the harmful planets, traditionally Saturn and Mars.</note>, it signifies bad profit. Regarding the cause for which one asks, it [the planet] rules over it. Know all this—

When someone asks you any interrogation, you shall begin to look just as I have told you before. I have already shown you in every case the manner in which it is determined; do not ever wish to stray in another way, nor confuse the cause of what is asked of you with the cause of what you are asking, nor place the question upon yourself, like one who asks about marriage.

¶ And when you speak there, look whether he asks you about another thing that seems to be commanded of him. And thus, in your **consideration** <note>The astrologer’s process of analyzing the chart.</note>, should you have thought of several diverse things, it is right and just that you take each thing from its own **chapter** <note>A specific category or rule set for a particular type of question (e.g., questions of love, money, or health).</note>; for it is not a proper thing to do if someone asks in a single interrogation about two things of the same kind.

<meta>This page continues the instructions for interpreting astrological questions (interrogations), specifically focusing on how the intent of the person asking the question relates to the planetary hours and the state of the heavens.</meta>

the intentions of men. For the consideration of them is made according to the wish and the intention of the **questioner** <note>original: "demandant"; the person seeking an astrological answer.</note>. He who asks and his intention will be reflected in that **interrogation** <note>term: "interrogation" — a specific branch of astrology (horary astrology) where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked.</note> throughout its duration. The hours of the planets signify, in the hour of interrogation, his state throughout his time. Similarly, if his intention is to ask about a certain thing regarding his year, or a month, or a day, or any such thing, it will be so. Understand therefore their intention and their consideration. For every questioner asks only about that which shines within him by the nature of the **circle** <note>The zodiac or the celestial sphere.</note> along with the state of that which he asks about. And the **complexion** <note>The overall "temperament" or quality of the astrological chart.</note> of the circle reflects his state, whether of good fortune or evil. There are branches and parts.

¶ To him, therefore, whom the fortune of the **lord** <note>The planet ruling a specific sign or house.</note> or of the **ascendant** <note>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question.</note> and of the moon corresponds, misfortune is shortened. And he to whom their **hindrance** <note>original: "empeschement"; an astrological affliction where a planet's power is weakened by a difficult aspect or position.</note> occurs is hindered, for he does not ask except in a state of hindrance of the **significator** <note>term: "significator" — the planet representing the person or the subject of the inquiry.</note>—that is, the moon and the lord of the ascendant—in the hour of interrogation, or in the **nativity** <note>The person's original birth chart.</note>; except for a man who is hindered, evil, and troubled, or a man to whom evil must come. Similarly, concerning fortune, no one asks about it except those who are entirely fortunate: a man destined to have fortune.

¶ Do not be ashamed if you ask about diverse things under one ascendant. For things are finite and diverse. For if many of them begin in one state—that is, in evil or in good, fortune or misfortune—it must happen. For we see some well-fortuned and others also fortuned.

That is, look to the one which will be heavier, and look at its freedom from those things I have mentioned to you before regarding the **hindrances**—

...movements of the planets. ¶ And if the question concerns the instability of a matter or a movement, such as a pilgrimage, a change, a release from prison <note>original: "issue de chartre"; literally 'exiting a dungeon'.</note>, or escaping the hand of tribulation, anguish, and distress—look at these things from the unstable place and the **reception** <term>reception — a relationship where one planet is in a sign ruled by another, suggesting cooperation</term>, that is, the **cadent houses** <note>original: "cheautes des angles"; these are the 3rd, 6th, 9th, and 12th houses, considered less powerful or "falling" away from the main angles of the chart.</note>. ¶ And if one asks you anything regarding the things that belong to the twelve signs, then the **ascendant** <note>The sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question.</note> and its **lord** <note>the planet that rules that specific zodiac sign.</note> and the moon shall be the **significators** <term>significator — the planet representing a person or object in an astrological chart</term> of the man who asks you; the sign of the matter and its lord signify the thing he seeks. And if you find the lord of the matter joined with the lord of the ascendant, that matter will be perfected easily and through the effort of the questioner, or through the petition itself, without any great difficulty. And if you find the lord of the ascendant or the moon in the house of the matter, or if you find the lord of the matter in the ascendant, the matter will be perfected—unless the ascendant is **hindered** <note>original: "empesche"; an astrological "affliction" where a planet's power is weakened by a difficult position.</note>, or the lord of the matter is in its **detriment** <note>original: "descente"; a weak position in the zodiac.</note> and its lord is also in its detriment—or if the lord of the matter is **combust** <term>combust — when a planet is so close to the Sun that its power is "burned up" or obscured</term> within it; in that case, it will not be accomplished. ¶

And if none of these things we have mentioned occur, then look to the **translation of light** <term>translation of light — when a fast-moving planet (like the moon) "carries" the influence from one planet to another through successive conjunctions</term> by the moon or by any of the light <note>Meaning fast-moving.</note> planets—and if you find it separating from the lord of the ascendant and joining the lord of the matter, or separating from the lord of the matter and joining the lord of the ascendant, the matter will be perfected through the hands of messengers <note>original: "legas"; envoys or intermediaries.</note> and those who travel between one person and another. ...who carries the light from one to its companion. Therefore, look equally at the light. ¶ And if you find the **lord** <note>the planet ruling a specific zodiac sign</note> of the matter and the lord of the **ascendant** <note>the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> both joined to a planet heavier <note>original: "plus pesante"; meaning a slower-moving planet like Jupiter or Saturn</note> than themselves, and that planet looks toward the place of the matter—whether it be in the ascendant or in the **midheaven** <note>the highest point in the chart, signifying success or authority</note>—the matter shall be **perfected** <term>perfected — brought to a successful completion</term> by the hands of a judge or a man to whom they are subject. Thus, by these three ways is the knowledge of all things made: 

First, from the **conjunction** <term>conjunction — when planets meet in the same degree of the zodiac</term> of the lord of the ascendant, the moon, and the lord of the matter. 

¶ Secondly, when some planet carries light between them; that is, it is separated from one of them and joined to the other. Then the matter is finished by the hands of **legates** <note>original: "legas"; meaning messengers or intermediaries</note>. 

¶ Thirdly, by the **collection of light**; that is, when they are both joined to some planet heavier than they are, which joins their light by taking the power of both. Its judgment will be acceptable between them, or through a man who will help them in that same matter. 

¶ Therefore, from these three chapters is the knowledge of things made.

That matter will be perfected; but if it is hindered, it will be destroyed after one has acquired it. And if the receiver is retrograde, the matter will be deceptive after one believes they have acquired it.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining the outcome of an astrological question, specifically moving from general rules to a practical case study. The previous page established how the "joining" or "separation" of planets determines whether a seeker's request will be fulfilled or destroyed.</meta>

...of these **interrogations** <note>original: "interrogatōns"; specific questions posed to an astrologer to determine the future</note> because they are fewer and suitable for all things.

<image-desc>A large ornamental gothic initial 'D' in blue ink, decorated with fine red penwork flourishes that extend vertically into the left margin and horizontally between lines. The flourishes include delicate loops and spiraling patterns.</image-desc>

**T**herefore, the example is an **interrogation** made for a kingdom, to know if it will be acquired or not. And the **ascendant** <term>ascendant — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question</term> was the 20th degree of **Gemini**. And the **Midheaven** <term>Midheaven — the highest point in the sky at that moment, representing the objective or "the matter" being asked about</term> was the 1st degree of **Pisces**. And the **Sun** <note>original: "soleil"</note> was in **Cancer** at 15 degrees. And **Mercury** <note>original: "mars"; likely a scribal error for Mercury, as it is the ruler of Gemini and is described as the "lord of the seeker" later</note> was in **Gemini** at 5 and 6 degrees. And **Mars** was in **Taurus** at the 9th degree. And **Venus** was in **Leo** at the 20th degree. **Jupiter** was in **Pisces** at the 20th degree in its **first station** <term>station — the period when a planet appears to stop moving before changing direction</term> and it was about to become **retrograde** <term>retrograde — the apparent backward movement of a planet across the sky</term>. And **Saturn** was in **Gemini** at the 9th degree. 

At that time, I looked in this interrogation at the **ascendant sign** and its **lord** <term>lord — the planet that rules a specific zodiac sign</term> and the **Moon**, which are the **significators** <term>significator — the planet representing a person or thing in an astrological chart</term> of the **seeker** <note>original: "demandant"; the person asking the question</note>. And [I looked] at the sign of the Midheaven and at its lord, which are the significators of the kingdom about which the interrogation was made. 

And Gemini was the ascendant, and **Mercury** <note>original: "mars"</note> was in the ascendant at the end of the sign. **Jupiter**, who was the lord of the matter, was at the Midheaven at the 20th degree. And I found the lord of the ascendant separating from the lord of the matter. I then looked at the Moon, which I found in the **angle of the earth** <term>angle of the earth — also known as the Imum Coeli or 4th House, representing roots, land, or the conclusion of an event</term> joined to Jupiter by **opposition** <term>opposition — an astrological aspect where planets are 180 degrees apart, usually signifying conflict or delayed success</term>. And this would signify the acquisition of the matter through impatience and labor, because they were joined by opposition. 

And if they had been joined by the **third aspect** <note>original: "3. ou tierce regart"; the trine aspect (120 degrees), which is considered harmonious and lucky</note>, the acquisition would have happened easily. And the Moon joining the lord of the matter would signify that this would be through the petition and the **effort** <note>original: "lestude"</note> of the seeker. And if it were the lord of the matter that had joined [the lord of the seeker], the acquisition of the matter would be easy through the initiative <note>original: "cōmencemēt"</note> of the one who would grant it, without petition or importunity from the seeker. ...death; and if the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant — the planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> in the **eighth** <note>original: "viij^e"; the 8th house, traditionally known as the House of Death</note> is regarded by an **evil** <term>evil — a "malefic" planet like Mars or Saturn, thought to bring hardship</term>, or if the Moon is **hindered** <note>original: "empeschee"; afflicted by bad planetary aspects</note>, then the patient will die. And the presence of the Lord of the 8th sign in the **angles** <term>angles — the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses, where planetary power is strongest</term> in a bad state—if the Lord of the Ascendant is not above him—if he be **joined** <note>in conjunction</note> to the Lord of the 8th [house] in the **house of death**, it signifies death. And if the Lord of the 8th does not **regard** <note>original: "regarde"; to be in an astrological aspect or geometric relationship</note> the Lord of the Ascendant, but some other planet transmits light between them, and the Lord of the Ascendant is **falling** <term>falling — also called "cadent"; a weak position in the chart</term>, and the Lord of the 8th is in an angle, this signifies death. And if the Lord of the Ascendant enters **combustion** <term>combustion — when a planet is within 12 degrees of the Sun, "burning" its power away</term> so that there are fewer than 12 degrees between it and the Sun, he will die likewise; and also if it is **adust** <term>adust — a state of being "scorched" by the Sun's heat, similar to combustion</term> and is not **received** <term>received — a supportive relationship where one planet is in a sign ruled by the other</term>. But if the planet is receiving the disposition, and if it be delivered from combustion, and the Moon is safe, this signifies **evasion** <note>recovery or escape from death</note>.

And if the house of sickness is a **movable sign** <term>movable sign — also called "cardinal" (Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn); they represent change and initiation</term>, the patient will be relieved at one time and aggravated at another. And if it is a **common sign** <term>common sign — also called "mutable" (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces); they represent transition</term>, he will change from one sickness to another. If it is a **fixed sign** <term>fixed sign — (Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, Aquarius); they represent stability and endurance</term>, the sickness will remain in one state. And if the Moon is separated from an **oriental planet** <term>oriental planet — a planet that rises before the Sun in the morning</term>, the sickness will be new and fresh. And if she is separated from a **western planet** <note>original: "occidentel"</note>, the sickness is already prolonged. And if she joins an oriental planet, this signifies the advancement of health. And if it is to a western planet, the sickness will be long. This is so much the better if **fortunes** <term>fortunes — "benefic" planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> regard the Lord of the Ascendant, provided it is in a good place relative to the Ascendant. The look of the **evils** upon it will be the worst if it is of the **fourth regard** <term>fourth regard — a square aspect (90 degrees), traditionally considered a sign of conflict</term> or of the **opposition** <note>an aspect of 180 degrees</note>, or if it is on the same line with it. And if the Moon is joined to a **retrograde** <term>retrograde — when a planet appears to move backward, symbolizing delays</term> planet, this signifies a long duration of sickness. And if she is joined to a slower planet, it aggravates continual pain, and it is doubtful whether he will die.

Know the place of the Moon on the first day of the beginning of the sickness, or of the **inquiry** <note>interrogacõn; the moment the doctor is asked to provide a prognosis</note>... on the 6th day, and the 11th, and the 18th...

On the day that the Moon shall come to the **malefic planets** <term>malefic planets — "evil" planets like Mars or Saturn, traditionally thought to cause harm</term> or to the Lord of the **house of sickness** <note>the 6th house in an astrological chart, which governs health and illness</note>, the patient will be **burdened** <note>original: "agrege"; meaning the illness will become heavier or more severe</note> on that day.
And when she [the Moon] shall come to the **benefics** <term>benefics — "fortunate" planets like Jupiter or Venus, thought to bring relief and healing</term> or when they shall **behold** <note>original: "regarderont"; when planets form a geometric aspect or relationship with one another</note> her, he will have health, rest, and [relief from] sickness. on the day the illness began or the inquiry [was made]. And on the **21st**

to her place—that is, to the sign and the degree in which she was at the

...of the **2nd house** <note>original: "·a·"; in this manuscript's shorthand, letters often designate specific houses</note> of the Ascendant, which is the **second of the house of servants** <note>The 2nd house from the 6th house (servants) represents the servant's personal property or money.</note>. Likewise, if the Lord of the 2nd is joined to the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant — the planet ruling the sign currently rising on the eastern horizon, representing the person asking the question</term>, or if it is in the Ascendant, or if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon is in the 2nd, or if you find a planet **carrying [light]** <term>translation of light — when a faster planet moves from a conjunction or aspect with one planet to another, "carrying" the influence between them</term> from one of them to its companion, he will acquire it.

And if one asks you about the possessions of a deceased servant, to know if they will come to the one who asks you or not: look at the **third sign from the house of servants** <note>original: "tiers signe... qui est · iij · de la maison des seruans"; likely referring to the 8th house of the total chart, which is the 3rd house from the 6th</note>. Likewise, if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon is in the third [from the servant's house], or the Lord of that third is in the Ascendant, or if there is a planet that transfers the light between them, he will acquire the possessions by the hands of **legates** <note>original: "legas"; intermediaries or legal representatives</note>.

**When you are asked about a marriage**, to know if it will be accomplished or not, and if it is done, what **covenant** <note>original: "couuenant"; the legal or social agreement of the marriage</note> shall be between them:

If you wish to know how the **judgment** <note>original: "separacōn"; here likely meaning the 'distinction' or 'determination' of the roles in the chart</note> is made, give the Ascendant and its Lord and the Moon to the **petitioner** <note>the person asking the question</note>, and the **7th sign** <note>original: "· d · signe"; the Descendant, or 7th house, which governs marriage and partners</note> and its Lord to the woman. If the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon is joined to the Lord of the **7th** <note>original: "· d ·"</note>, or if it is in the 7th, he shall have the woman. This is also the case if the Lord of the 7th is joined to the Lord of the Ascendant, or if it is in the Ascendant; the matter will be accomplished easily, and it will be more by the will of the woman than of the man. 

And if the Moon carries light between them, **messengers** <note>original: "legas"</note> will go between them and the matter will be accomplished by their hands. After this, look at the **receiver of the disposition** <term>receiver — the planet that "receives" another into its own zodiacal signs, showing support or acceptance</term>; this is the highest planet. If it is hindered by the "evil ones" <note>unfortunate planets like Saturn or Mars</note> by a **square aspect** <note>original: "quart regart"; an 90-degree angle indicating tension or obstacles</note> or by opposition, or if it is **cadent** <term>cadent — "falling" houses (3rd, 6th, 9th, 12th) where planets are considered weaker</term>, the matter will be destroyed rather than settled. And if you are asked in what manner the destruction shall happen, look at the "evil" planet that hinders: if it is the Lord of the 2nd house of the Ascendant or of the **7th** <note>original: "· g ·"; here likely shorthand for the 7th house or its gains</note>, the destruction [will be financial].

### According to the question of a woman who has gone away
<note>original: "Selen te demande d'une fame"</note>
If you are asked about a woman who has gone away because of the **anger** <note>original: "l'ire"</note> of her husband, to know if she will return to her house or not: look at **Venus** and the **Sun**, who are the **significators** <term>Significators — planets that represent the specific people or things involved in an astrological question</term> of the man and the woman. If Venus is situated in a very good place in the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant — the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question</term>, and the Sun is above the **earth** <note>original: "sur la t’re [terre]"; likely meaning above the horizon</note>, say that the woman will return after a great and long delay. 

And if the **Moon** is in the [position she was in] at the hour she left her house, or at the hour of the **interrogation** <term>Interrogation — the specific moment a question is posed to an astrologer to be answered by a chart</term>, and if she has passed the **prevention** <note>original: "preuētiōu"; likely referring to the previous syzygy or lunar phase</note> and after half a month, she will return to the house quickly. If the **Moon** is increasing in light—that is, at the beginning of the month—she will return very late. 

And if **Venus** is **retrograde** <term>Retrograde — when a planet appears to move backward in the sky, often suggesting a reversal or return</term> from her house and **occidental** <term>Occidental — setting in the west after the sun, which can indicate the "timing" or "state" of a person</term>, the return to her house will be of her own will, and she will repent. If she is occidental, emerging from under the **rays** <note>original: "sous les rays"; meaning the planet is no longer 'combust' or hidden by the sun's glare</note> and [the Sun] looks at her, she will return and her husband will repent for her leaving; but she will not repent for what she has done (as she repents in the first chapter), but she will repent for the return.

### According to the question of whether a woman is a virgin or corrupted
<note>original: "virge ou corrūpue"</note>
Look at the **Ascendant** at the hour you are asked, and its **Lord**, and the **Moon**. If it is in **fixed signs** <term>Fixed signs — Taurus, Leo, Scorpio, and Aquarius; in this context, they suggest stability and a lack of "movement" or change in status</term>, she will be a pure virgin, free of all vice and cleared of the one by whom she is slandered. And if it is in **movable** or **common signs** <term>Movable and Common signs — Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn (movable) and Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces (common); these suggest changeability or previous "action"</term>, she will be corrupted and a wife. 

If the woman believes herself to be a virgin, she is mocking [the truth] and is so no longer. And if the **Moon** is in a **common** or **movable sign**, and the **Ascendant** and its Lord are in a **fixed sign**, she has already been **labored** <note>original: "traueillhe"; a euphemism for sexual activity</note> before, and her virginity is not well-proven. And if the **Moon** is with **Mars** in a **movable** or **common sign**, her virginity is already taken for the love of men. And if **Saturn** is in the **Ascendant** with the...
