<meta>This page continues the specific astrological instructions for "interrogations" (divinatory questions) regarding the status and conduct of women. The previous page discussed the return of a runaway wife; this page focuses on determining a woman's sexual history (virginity), her children, and her fidelity to her husband based on the positions of the Moon, Venus, and Mars.</meta>

If the **Moon** is in a **common sign** <term>Common sign — also known as mutable signs (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, and Pisces), which represent change and transition</term> or a dry one, the man who was joined to her used her against reason and nature, and she did not have her virginity taken [rightly]. And if **Mars** is in an **angle** <term>Angle — the four most powerful points in an astrological chart, representing the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</term> of **Venus**, which is a **quartile aspect** <note>original: "quart regart"; an aspect of 90 degrees, usually considered stressful</note> or in the degree of **Venus**, and the **Moon** is hindered by **Mars** and is in **Scorpio** <note>original: "scorpio"</note> or in its **triplicity** <term>Triplicity — a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water)</term>, she will not be a virgin. But if you find **Mars** or **Jupiter** in the **triplicity** of **Venus** or in her **angle**, and **Mars** is falling away sharply from her and does not aspect her, and **Mars** is in **Aries** <note>original: "aries"</note>, **Leo** <note>original: "leo"</note>, or **Sagittarius** <note>original: "sagitari⁹"</note>, she will be a virgin.

And if you are asked about a woman, whether she has a son or not: look at **Venus**; if she is in **Aquarius** <note>original: "aquarius"</note> or **Leo**, or if **Mars** is with her, she never gave birth. And if **Venus** and **Mars** are in **Scorpio** or **Taurus** <note>original: "tauri⁹"</note>, she has a son. And if **Mars** and the **Moon** or **Venus** are in a **common sign**, except in **Sagittarius**, she has a son; for **Sagittarius** signifies that the woman does not have a son and that she never gave birth—but if she does give birth to a son, he will die.

And if the **malefics** <term>Malefics — planets traditionally thought to bring challenges or misfortune, specifically Saturn and Mars</term> <note>original: "les malvaises"</note> are in **moveable signs** <term>Moveable signs — also known as cardinal signs (Aries, Cancer, Libra, and Capricorn), associated with action and change</term>, her son will be from **fornication** <note>In this context, children born outside of a legal marriage</note> and through friendship she does not keep faith with her husband.

And if the **benefics** <term>Benefics — planets traditionally thought to bring good luck or positive outcomes, specifically Jupiter and Venus</term> <note>original: "les fortunes"</note> are in **common signs**, her son will be of **loyalty** <note>meaning legitimate or born within the marriage</note>. And if the two fortunes—that is, the good and the bad [planets]—are mixed in **moveable signs**, she will have sadness because of her son. And if you wish to know if the lost thing shall be found or not, look to the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant — the planet ruling the zodiac sign that was rising on the eastern horizon at the moment the question was asked</term> and the Moon. Similarly, [this is true] if any planet joins their light, for instance if both are joined to any planet more powerful than themselves. 

And if the Lord of the Second [House], who is the significator of the substance <note>original: "ſubſtance"; referring to the stolen goods or physical property</note>, is in the **Ascendant**, this signifies acquisition after a little time. Similarly, [if the Lord of the Ascendant] is joined to the planet which is in the second sign from the Ascendant; and if it does not **aspect** <term>Aspect — a specific geometric angle between planets, described here as "looking at" (regarde) one another</term> the Lord of the Ascendant, the substance shall perish <note>original: "perira"; meaning it will be destroyed or lost beyond recovery</note>. 

Similarly, if the Lord of the degree <note>original: "le. gre."; likely referring to the specific degree of the Ascendant or a calculated "Lot"</note> of the Ascendant [is joined] to the Lord of the **Fourth [House]** <term>Fourth House — the area of the chart representing the "end of matters" and, in theft cases, the thief's hiding place</term>, who is the significator of the thief, and the Lord of the Fourth is joined to the Lord of the Second, this signifies the loss <note>original: "pte"; abbreviation for "perte"</note> of the substance. 

And if the Lord of the degree, who is the significator of the thief's substance, is joined to the Lord of the Second, who is the significator of the substance of the petitioner <note>original: "demandeur"; the person asking the astrologer to find their goods</note>, he shall find the substance which he lost, and take a portion of the thief's substance as well, and so much more if the Lord of the Ascendant aspects them. 

¶ For if the Lord of the degree [is aspected by] the King <note>referring to the Lord of the Tenth House or Midheaven, representing legal authority</note>, he shall take that which I have said. ¶ And if the Lord of the **Midheaven** <term>Midheaven — original: "demi le ciel," the highest point in the sky, representing the judge or the King's power</term>, who is the significator of the King, [reaches] the thief, the King [shall act] against [the thief]. And if the Lord of the Second of the Ascendant does not aspect the Ascendant, according to the signs, the thing shall be entirely lost and never mentioned again. 

¶ And if the Lord of the Second of the Ascendant [is joined] to the Lord of the **Third** or the **Fourth [House]**, or to any planet within them, or if the Lord of the Second is in them, this signifies that the thing which was stolen <note>original: "emblee"</note> shall leave the region. 

And know that if the **two luminaries** <term>Two luminaries — the Sun and the Moon</term> are beneath the earth <note>located in the houses below the horizon line (houses 1 through 6)</note>, one shall never know the identity of the thief.

...is taken or lost, or which has been stolen on that day, it shall be found and will return more quickly and all the more easily if it [the significator] aspects <note>original: "regart"; the geometric relationship between planets</note> [the Ascendant].

And if you wish to know if the thief is a stranger, a third party, or not: look to the **Lights** <term>Lights — the Sun and the Moon</term>. And if both aspect the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant — the zodiac sign that was rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the inquiry, representing the victim</term>, the thief shall be of the household. And if the Ascendant aspects one of them but not the other, he shall be mixed among them but is not of the household <note>original: "mesle entre eulx"; implying an acquaintance or someone known to the family but not living there</note>.

Similarly, if you find the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant — the planet that rules the rising sign</term> in the Ascendant, or if the **Lord of the Fourth [House]** <term>Fourth House — the section of the chart representing the home, family, and hidden things</term> is with the Lord of the Ascendant, the thief shall be among the inhabitants of the house <note>original: "lostel"; the residence or household</note>. And if the Lights are in the houses of the Lord of the Ascendant, or in their own houses aspecting the Ascendant or its Lord, the thief shall be an inhabitant of the house. 

And if the Lights are in their **triplicities** <term>Triplicities — a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, or Water)</term>, the thief shall be one of those close to them, but he does not live with them in the house. And if they are in their **terms** <term>Terms — specific subdivisions of a zodiac sign ruled by different planets</term> or **faces** <term>Faces — also known as decans; ten-degree divisions of a zodiac sign</term>, he will be an associate of the inhabitants of the house. And it is believed that there was previously a closeness between them <note>The text suggests a prior intimacy or "prochainete" between the thief and the victims</note>. 

And if they aspect the Ascendant and do not aspect the Fourth [House], he had never entered the house before that time, unless one of the Lights is in a **common sign** <term>Common Sign — also called "mutable" signs (Gemini, Virgo, Sagittarius, Pisces), which represent change and instability</term>. In that case, he has already entered it, but the inhabitants knew nothing of it, for the common sign signifies betrayal <note>original: "trahison"</note>. 

And if one of the Lights aspects the Lord of the Ascendant, he will be known to the inhabitants of the house, but he had not entered the house before. And if the Lord of the Ascendant has already fallen from the degree of the Ascendant <note>meaning the planet has moved just past the specific point of the horizon</note> and another planet is with it in a sign where it is closer to the degree of the Ascendant, the thief was previously an inhabitant of the house. 

And if the Lord of the Fourth is five degrees from its house, the thief will not be from that region. And if it is six degrees or ten degrees away, it will be a servant of the house or a chambermaid. And if it is in its **exaltation** <term>Exaltation — a sign where a planet’s influence is at its most powerful and dignified</term>, the thief will be a nobleman. And if it is in its own house, he will be one of the inhabitants of the house and known to everyone.

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for identifying a thief using horary astrology. It transitions from determining if the thief is a household member to specifying exactly which family member or associate is responsible based on the planetary significators.</meta>

If he <note>the planet representing the thief</note> is in his **triplicity** <term>Triplicity — a group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element (Fire, Earth, Air, Water) which share a common planetary ruler</term>, or in his **term** <term>Term — a specific segment of a zodiac sign ruled by a planet, used to measure a planet's strength</term>, or in his **face** <term>Face — also known as a decan, a ten-degree division of a zodiac sign</term>, he shall not be known throughout his region, but he will be known in his street and in his dwelling. 

And when you know that the thief is of the household <note>original: "lostel"</note>, and the **Sun** <note>original: "solail"</note> is the significator of that house—that is, of the **Fourth House** <note>indicated by the abbreviation ".a." in the manuscript, referring to the house of the home and family</note>—it shall be his father. If it is the **Moon**, it shall be his mother. If it is **Jupiter**, it shall be his wife. If it is **Saturn**, it shall be his son <note>original: "fuist," likely a variant of "fils"</note> or a stranger. And if it is **Jupiter** <note>original: "jupit'"</note>, he shall be the most noble of all those in the household, and one who is not known for theft, of whom there was no suspicion. And if it is **Mars**, it shall be the son or the daughter or his brother. And if it is **Mercury**, it shall be among his close friends. 

And if the **significator** <term>Significator — the planet that represents a specific person or thing in an astrological chart</term> of the thief is a stranger, look to the **Part of Fortune** <term>Part of Fortune — a mathematical point in a chart calculated from the positions of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant, representing luck and physical reality</term>; if it is free from "evil" <note>afflictions from harsh planets like Saturn or Mars</note>, the thief has never stolen anything before this time. Similarly, if the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant — the planet that rules the zodiac sign that was rising at the time of the theft</term> is free from evils, and if **Mars** is separated from the Lord of the Fourth House, he [the thief] was well known to them before.

And if **Saturn** aspects the Moon or the **Ascendant** <term>Ascendant — the rising sign, representing the victim</term>, the thief stole it through subtlety and craftiness <note>original: "cautelle"</note>. And if **Jupiter** is the significator of the thief—that is, if he is the Lord of the Fourth House—he did not enter the house to steal, but entered for some other business; by chance <note>original: "de accident"</note> he saw the object and then stole it. 

And if **Mars** is the significator, the theft will not have occurred without him having broken into <note>original: "percie"</note> the house, or having tunneled through the wall of the house where the thing was, or having forced the windows or broken the locks <note>original: "cles"; literally keys</note>. And if **Venus** is the **signifier** <note>original: "signefieresse"; the feminine form of the word</note>, this signifies friendship, companionship, and boldness between them; he entered to please them and feigned that he came to visit as a friend, and thus he stole from them. 

And if **Mars** is the significator, the thief entered the house by ingenuity <note>original: "engin"</note> and craftiness or by some trick. For when the **Sun** and the **Moon** aspect the Ascendant, the thief shall be one of the inhabitants of the household. And if the significator of the thief is **fortunate** <note>a "benefic" planet like Jupiter or Venus</note>, he will be a free man, and if he is **malefic** <note>original: "maluais," like Saturn or Mars</note>, he shall be a servant.

And if he has taken nothing from the malefics, he has stolen nothing.

### The King or the Captain of the Host.

...[observa]tion; if it returns to the degree of **combustion** <term>combustion — when a planet is so close to the Sun (within 8.5 degrees) that its power is said to be "burnt up" or weakened</term>, he shall be dead. And if the Moon is joined to a planet below the earth <note>in the houses currently below the horizon</note>, he shall be dead. And if she is joined to a planet above the earth, he shall be alive. 

And if you find the lord of the **ascendant** <note>the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> in the 12th [house] with the **malefics** <term>malefics — planets like Saturn or Mars, traditionally called "the bad ones" (mauves) because they were thought to bring difficulty</term>, or if the malefics look upon it <note>form an astrological aspect to it</note>, and either of the **luminaries** <note>the Sun or the Moon</note> is afflicted, you shall judge him dead. And if the malefics are in a line with the lights—which are the Sun and the Moon—without the aspect of the **benefics** <term>benefics — planets like Jupiter or Venus, called "fortunes" because they bring help and good luck</term>, it signifies death. 

And similarly, if the Moon is with Mars in a **square aspect** <note>original: "u quart"; an angle of 90 degrees, considered a sign of conflict</note> and the benefics do not look upon her. And similarly, if the Moon [or] the **Part of Fortune** <term>Part of Fortune — a specific point in a chart calculated based on the positions of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant</term> is with the malefics in a square to the ascendant, or in the 6th or 12th [house], and the benefics do not look upon it. 

And know that what is above the earth signifies life, and what is below the earth signifies death. Therefore, if you find the lord of the ascendant **combust** under the [Sun's] rays, and no benefic looks upon it, and the Moon is below the earth, falling from the ascendant in the 3rd or 6th [house], know that the one about whom you are asked is dead. And especially if it so happens that the Moon is in the sign of **Scorpio** at its third degree, afflicted by **Saturn**—for naturally this signifies death—then you shall judge the death of the one about whom the **interrogation** <note>original: "interrogacion"; a specific question put to an astrologer to be answered by a chart</note> is made.

### On Pilgrimages and Journeys

And if you are asked about a **pilgrimage** <note>original: "pegrination" or "pelerinage"; this refers to any long journey, often for religious or spiritual purposes</note>, whether it shall happen or not, and if it cannot be known what thing awaits him: look at the lord of the ascendant and the Moon, which are the significators of the petitioner <note>the person asking the question</note>, and the 9th sign <note>the 9th house, which governs long travel</note> and its lord, which are the significators of the pilgrimage. 

If the lord of the ascendant or the Moon is in the 9th [house], or if one of them is joined to the lord of the 9th, he who asks about the pilgrimage shall never have profit nor honor. And if the lord of the 9th is in the ascendant and is joined to the lord of the ascendant, he shall have such a journey that in no way can he be frustrated nor fail to finish. And if it is not so...

Of the lord of the **ascendant** <note>the planet ruling the sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question</note> and of the 9th [house], as I have said: if some planet returns their light to them together, this is a **translation of light** <term>translation of light — when a fast-moving planet connects two slower planets by moving from an aspect with one to an aspect with the other</term>. This signifies **pilgrimage** <note>original: "perigrinatōn"</note>. And if this is not the case, but you find the lord of the ascendant and the lord of the 9th joined to a heavier <note>slower-moving</note> planet, and that planet looks upon the house of pilgrimage <note>the 9th house, which governs long journeys</note>, this signifies pilgrimage. And if it does not look upon it, he will not go on pilgrimage.

¶ **And** if the lord of the ascendant is in an **angle** <term>angle — the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, considered the strongest positions in a chart</term>, and it is joined to a planet that is in the left part of the ascendant—which is between the ascendant and the third [house]—and it is not bothered by the **malefics** <note>harmful planets like Saturn or Mars</note>, this signifies pilgrimage. And if the lord of the ascendant and the Moon [are joined] to any planet in an angle, he will not go on pilgrimage. And if the significators are joined together—that is, the lord of the ascendant and the lord of the 9th—and there is in the ascendant some malefic star that hinders the lord of the ascendant and the lord of the 9th, there will be no pilgrimage, and this will happen because of the person who asks. 

Something will happen today that will disturb him from his pilgrimage. And if the malefic is [in the house of the destination], the trouble will come to him from that land where he wished to go, or from the thing he was asking about, which will take away his pilgrimage.

¶ **And** if the malefic is in the **midheaven** <term>midheaven — the 10th house, representing the highest point in the sky and those in power</term>, the hindrance will come to him from the king or from someone who is above him. And if the lord of the ascendant is joined to the lord of the house of the journey <note>the 9th house</note>, and then it is joined to a malefic by **opposition** <note>180 degrees apart</note> or **conjunction** <note>occupying the same degree</note>, or by a **square aspect** <note>original: "quart regard"; 90 degrees apart</note>, or if it is with it in one sign: 

judge the distress that will come to him after the pilgrimage according to the nature of that planet. That is, if it is the lord of the 6th [house] from the ascendant, he will have illness; and if it is the lord of the 4th, he will have imprisonment <note>original: "chartre"</note> or some sadness. And if it is the lord of the 8th, it will be death. 

¶ **And** if it is the lord of the 11th or 12th, this signifies distress from thieves and dangerous adventures and evil. 

¶ **And** if it looks upon the ascendant, there is fear that he may be killed.

If one asks you on what he will spend the riches he has **acquired**, look at the Lord of the **House of Riches** <note>the 2nd house</note>. 

If it is **Mars**, he will spend and expend it on every evil work, such as war, lust, and drunkenness, and such things. 

And if it is **Saturn**, he will have anguish in his heart, and strangers will devour his goods. 

And if it is **Jupiter**, he will spend it for the love of God on alms and on every good work. 

And if it is the **Sun**, he will spend it for the sake of his people and to acquire nobility. 

And if it is **Venus**, he will use it in his heart for every delight <note>original: "delectation", referring to sensual or aesthetic pleasures</note>. 

And if it is **Mercury**, it will be spent on buying or selling in the pursuit of **gain**. 

If it is the **Moon**, she will work according to the nature of the planet <note>the Moon "reflects" the nature of the planet she is most closely connected to at that time</note>.

If the malefic planet that hinders the **Lord of the Ascendant** <term>Lord of the Ascendant — the planet that rules the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of an event, representing the ruler’s person</term> is **Saturn**, then what I said regarding distress and martyrdom shall come to pass through imprisonment and by being beaten. ¶ And if **Mars** is the hindering planet, it shall be through chains and irons and the striking of flails <note>The text likely refers to physical punishment or torture</note>. ¶ And observe the hour of his entry into his kingdom and onto his throne; when he sits upon the seat of his empire and begins to command and govern, if his entry occurs by day and the **Sun** is with the **Ascendant**, he shall soon be deposed from that office. [commence]ment of his principality <term>principalte — the period or nature of his rule</term>. ¶ And if you find the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the Midheaven in the 12th or the 6th [houses] <note>The 12th and 6th houses were traditionally associated with enemies, illness, and misfortune</note>, judge that his ending shall be swift, unless **Jupiter** is oriental <term>oriaentel — rising before the Sun, a position of strength</term> in the Ascendant or in the Midheaven; for this causes the dissolution and downfall to be delayed.

After this, observe the sign of the Midheaven and the Lord [of the house] where he is, and who aspects <note>original: "regarde," referring to the geometric angles planets form with one another</note> him. If the Lord of the Midheaven is in an "angle" <term>langle — one of the four most powerful houses in a chart (1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th)</term> and the benefics <term>fortunes — favorable planets, usually Jupiter and Venus</term> aspect him, this signifies the goodness of his state. And if a benefic is in the Midheaven, he will come in this undertaking to a better and more worthy state through trust and willpower, and he shall be of great dignity in his honor, and his enterprise shall be illustrious, and his gain shall be multiplied.

And if a malefic holds itself there, he will find great distress in that thing, and everything he gains there will be taken from him with great suffering, and through weeping and desolation caused by the king he will have against him.

And if a benefic aspects the Midheaven and there is no malefic, his work will be praiseworthy. If a malefic aspects it, his work will be destroyed.

¶ And if there is an evil influence <note>original: "annime maluaise," literally "evil soul," likely referring to the planet's essential nature</note> and a benefic aspects it, observe which of them is stronger and has more degrees <note>Planets with higher degrees within a sign or greater "essential dignity" were considered to have more power over the outcome</note>, for the stronger one overcomes the other, and the other will take some part of him.

¶ And if **Mars** is with **Jupiter** in the Midheaven, his undertaking will have great abundance through wisdom, counsel, [provision] <note>original: "inison," possibly a variant of 'provision' or 'union'</note>, and ingenuity; and through this he will have a great name and great renown, and he will be established over his work, his lordship, and these regions, especially if he aspects the **Moon**.

¶ But if the **Sun** is in the Midheaven with them without the aspect of a malefic, this sign...

**And also, this shall be when Jupiter is with the Moon in the Midheaven and Venus is in a very good place.** <note>The Midheaven, or 10th house, represents career and public status. Having "benefics" like Jupiter and Venus there was considered highly auspicious.</note> **And whenever you find a "fortune"** <term>fortune — a "benefic" or lucky planet, such as Jupiter or Venus</term> **in the Midheaven, his distress shall be diminished and he shall have good; and if a "malefic"** <term>mauvaise — an unlucky planet, like Mars or Saturn</term> **is there, and the Lord of the Midheaven and the Lord of the Ascendant are "cadent"** <term>cheans — planets in houses that "fall away" from the powerful angles, usually indicating weakness</term>, **he shall last but a little time in his work.**

**And whenever you find Mars in the 5th degree of the Ascendant and a "fortune" is not in the Ascendant, he shall die in that work.** **And if Saturn is there and a "fortune" is in the Ascendant or in the 5th degree, he shall not die nor be killed in that work.** **And if a "fortune" is in the 5th degree, he shall be deposed very gracefully, with safety and health.**

**And if a "fortune" is in the fourth house** <note>original: "u quart"; the 4th house represents the end of matters and the grave</note> **and Saturn is in conjunction** <note>original: "u corps," meaning "in the body" or sharing the same degree</note>, **the end shall be according to his wishes.** **And if a "malefic" is there, he shall find evil and hindrance after he is deposed from his work, with great suffering, imprisonment** <term>chartre — literally a dungeon or jail</term>, **distress, and a long time in prison.** **And if a "fortune" aspects the fourth house, he shall escape after the tribulation and distress he finds; and if a "fortune" does not aspect the "malefic" that is in the fourth house, he shall be bound and suffer great pains and die in prison.** **But if the Lord of the fourth house is in the Ascendant or in the 10th house or in those other very good places, even if the ruler be born of ill-fortune** <note>original: "nait de mauvais," likely referring to an unlucky natal chart</note>, **and the Lord of the Moon is similarly placed, this signifies a good end to that same work.**

**And when you are asked about the king’s staying or his departure, look at the Lord of the Ascendant and the Lord of the 10th house. If they are joined and the "receiver of the disposition"** <note>An astrological technique where one planet "hands over" its influence to another</note> — **which is the heavier** <term>pesant — referring to the slower-moving planet, like Jupiter or Saturn</term> **of them — is in an "angle," that king shall not move nor leave his kingdom.** **And if the receiver of the disposition is to the left of the Ascendant, he shall go away but he shall return.** **And if the receiver of the disposition is received in...**

If the seeker <note>original: "le leu," likely referring to the person asking the question</note> asks you about a letter to know if it will come to the king or not, look at the Masters <term>Masters — the planetary "Lords" or rulers of specific signs and houses</term> to see if he is joined to the Lord of the Midheaven or to the Sun; say that it will arrive, and if not, not.

If he is in the angles—whether he be beneficial or malicious—and provided he is not joined to a falling planet <term>falling planet — a 'cadent' planet, one located in a house that 'falls away' from the angles, often signifying weakness</term>, the news will be true. And if he is in an angle but joined to a falling planet that does not "receive" <term>reception — an astrological relationship where one planet is in a sign or dignity ruled by another, suggesting cooperation or support</term> him, mention has been made of this news, but it will not be fulfilled <note>original: "pfaites," or perfected; in astrology, a 'perfected' aspect means the event actually happens</note>.

¶ And if the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to a malicious planet that does not receive him, this signifies the destruction of the news, and this will be caused by the seeker <note>original: "le signour du demandant," literally the Lord of the one demanding the answer</note>.

And Mars receives the Sun. For the Sun is in the house of Mars <note>The Sun is actually exalted in Aries, which is the "house" or domicile of Mars. Similarly, for the other four planets, whichever of them is joined to its companion—that is, to another planet—within its house or its exaltation through any of the known aspects <note>original: "regars cogneuz"; these are the geometric angles like squares or trines that planets form with one another</note>, or is joined in a single line <note>a conjunction</note>; and it "casts"—that is to say, it commits its disposition to it <term>Disposition: In medieval astrology, this is the way one planet hands over its "will" or influence to the planet that rules the sign it currently occupies.</term>—if the planet to whom the disposition is committed receives it, it brings the matter to completion by the command of God.

<image-desc>blue decorative paraph mark</image-desc> And if the Sun were in the first degree of the Balance <note>Libra</note> and Saturn in the 30th degree of the Ram <note>Aries</note>, and no other planet is in the Ram nor in any other aspect closer than the Sun is to the conjunction of Saturn; and provided that Saturn does not leave the Ram until the Sun is joined to it degree by degree; then when the Sun is joined to Saturn degree by degree, the Sun receives Saturn and Saturn receives the Sun. For in this place each receives the other through exaltation. For the Sun is in the exaltation of Saturn <note>Saturn is traditionally "exalted" or highly powerful in the sign of Libra</note>, and Saturn is in the exaltation of the Sun <note>The Sun is "exalted" in the sign of Aries</note>. And if such a reception occurs in opposition or in the fourth aspect <note>a square, or 90-degree angle, often considered difficult</note>, it signifies envy, labor, anxiety, and restraint. And in the third aspect <note>a trine, or 120-degree angle</note> and in the sixth <note>a sextile, or 60-degree angle</note> and in conjunction, it signifies ease, pity, and subtlety.

And as the Sun shall be joined to Saturn, and Saturn shall receive the Sun and be received by the Sun through their houses or exaltations, they shall be reconciled and the cause shall be perfected by the command of God. Similarly, all fortunate planets <note>original: "fortunes"; these are the "benefics" like Jupiter and Venus which were thought to bring good luck</note> joined with fortunate ones increase the good. And evil planets <note>original: "maluez"; these are "malefics" like Saturn and Mars, traditionally associated with hardship</note> joined with evil ones are made good through reception—that is to say, they are well placed, or they cause their hindrance and evil to depart. And fortunate planets with evil planets are reconciled, and their evil departs, and the matter is perfected.

### Concerning the first house regarding the state of life and death of the petitioner: whether he shall die in the current year or whether he shall escape

If you look at the man who asks you about [his] life—namely, whether he shall die in that year or not—you shall look at him as you would look at a child; that is to say, look at to whom the **Lord of the Ascendant** is joined. 

And if the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to a **fortunate** planet <term>fortune: a "benefic" planet, traditionally Jupiter or Venus, which were thought to bring positive outcomes</term> that is the **dispositor** <note>original: "desposerresse"; the planet that rules the sign currently occupied by another planet</note> to which the disposition has come, and if it does not pass that disposition to another; and also, provided that planet is not the Mistress of the **House of Death** <note>The eighth house of the zodiac, which governs death and inheritance.</note>, say that he shall not die in that year, by the command of God. 

And if the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to a **malice** planet <term>maluez: a "malefic" planet, traditionally Saturn or Mars, associated with hardship</term> or to the Lord of the **House of Death**—whatever planet it may be, whether malevolent or fortunate, provided it is the Lord of the House of Death—and if that Lord of the Ascendant is not received, nor does it re[ceive]...

And if the day on which the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to that malefic planet has not yet arrived, it shall be when the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to that planet **by its body** <note>original: "p[ar] son corps"; a physical conjunction where two planets reach the same longitudinal degree.</note> and shall be first at its degree. And as he shall be joined to it by his body, degree by degree, then he shall die.

But if the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to a planet that aids him, then the sick person or the infirmity shall escape death in this same year by the command of God, and the sick person shall be healed of the infirmity by the command of God. ¶ And if the Lord of the Ascendant is further hindered by the **Moon**, just as I told you before regarding the Lord of the Ascendant, then judge by her just as you judged by the Lord of the Ascendant. 

And if after the infirmity, prosperity and health accompany him, look at when the Lord of the Ascendant shall be joined degree by degree to the planet that signifies his health. And these degrees—that is to say, the degrees of that **conjunction** <term>conjunction: An astrological aspect where two planets occupy the same position in the sky, blending their powers.</term>—set as days. And if health is not then [present], it shall be when the Lord of the Ascendant is joined to that planet by its body or the line where it is. And if he is joined to it in the preceding place or in one of the **angles** <term>angles: The four most powerful points in a horoscope: the Ascendant, Midheaven, Descendant, and Imum Coeli.</term>, or if he is already distant from it, then he shall escape if God wills.—

Know also that when the Lord of the Ascendant or the **Moon** is joined to a fortunate planet, or even to a malevolent planet that receives it, the sick person will not cease to find relief <note>original: "de legier"; literally to grow lighter or be eased of a burden.</note> until that planet **commits its disposition** <term>commit disposition: A process where one planet "hands over" its power or the outcome of a situation to another planet through an astrological connection.</term> to act degree by degree. And if that planet commits its disposition to a malevolent planet that does not receive it at all, and which is the Lord of the House of Death—and if the disposition comes from him and no other—this means that by committing his disposition to him, the sick person will die. And if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon is separated from the planet that received it, and that planet has transferred its disposition to a malevolent planet that does not receive it, and which is the Lord of the House of Death...

The **conjunction** <term>conjunction: When two planets appear at the same point in the zodiac, suggesting a merging of their influences.</term> of the **lord of the ascendant** <note>The planet ruling the zodiac sign that was rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question; it represents the person asking the question.</note> or of the **Moon** with the lord of the "thing signified" <note>In this context, the "thing" is the wealth or property being asked about.</note> signifies the acquisition of the thing by one's own efforts. For **reception** governs these matters. And it cannot be but that the thing be done sooner or later; however, the **angles** hasten and confirm the matter. And the **succedent houses** <note>original: "secondes maisons apres." These are the houses (2nd, 5th, 8th, 11th) following the angles, considered moderately powerful.</note> are likewise profitable. But the **cadent houses** <note>original: "maisons eschantés." And if the signifier of the ascendant or the Moon—that is to say, either of them—be joined to the lord of the house of substance, or to a benefic in the house of substance, or to a malefic planet that has a "part" (that is to say, **dignity** <term>dignity: A planet's strength based on its position in a specific zodiac sign where it has traditional rights or "authorship."</term>) in the house of substance; [the matter will succeed] even if that malefic does not receive the lord of the ascendant. Or, [if it be joined] to a malefic planet in the house of substance which has no part (that is to say, dignity) in that same house, provided that it receives the lord of the ascendant, the thing shall be.

And if the lord of the second house (which is the house of substance) or a planet being in that same house be joined to the lord of the ascendant, the thing is accomplished without <insert>any seeking</insert>. And it will be perfected by the pleasure of God. The conjunction of the lord of the ascendant and of the Moon with planets signifies the petition for the thing made by the **interrogation** <note>original: "interogacion." This refers to the formal act of asking the astrologer the question.</note> before it is actually granted.

If anyone asks you about a good or an evil thing—whether it exists, whether it is lasting, or whether it is yet to come—and you find the planets that have **dominion** <term>dominion: Also known as 'lordship'; the planet that rules the specific zodiac sign or 'house' related to the question.</term> over the matter in the **angles** <term>angles: The four most powerful points in an astrological chart: the Ascendant (East), Midheaven (South), Descendant (West), and Imum Coeli (North).</term>; then that good or evil will be lasting. ...[unless] Mars had cut the **conjunction** <term>conjunction: When two planets reach the same degree of the zodiac, merging their influences.</term> between the Sun and Saturn.

## Here begins the treatise of Mash'allah to Valens <note>original: "Messehallath a uales". Likely referring to the Hellenistic astrologer Vettius Valens, though medieval texts often paired famous names together pseudo-epigraphically.</note> to know the thought and contemplation of one who comes to [the astrologer]

<image-desc>A vertical decorative border runs down the left margin, featuring delicate red and blue pen-flourished scrollwork and floral motifs. A large, ornate illuminated capital 'M' in the body text is decorated with blue and red patterns and fine white penwork, set against a background of intricate scrolling lines.</image-desc>

Mash'allah commanded that before all other things you must establish the **ascendant** <term>The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the exact moment a question is asked.</term> by its degree and its minute, and also the other **houses** <term>Twelve divisions of the sky representing different areas of life, such as wealth, siblings, or health.</term> very precisely. And he says that **interrogations** <term>A branch of astrology, also called "horary," where a chart is cast to answer a specific question.</term> are of three types: the first is to know for what cause the person who comes has come and what thing they ask for; the second is to know what will be the cause of their inquiry; the third is to know if the interrogation is perfect or not and what end it will have. **Therefore, when you desire to know what I shall tell you, in order to know it, look at the ascendant and its lord, and the Moon and the lord of her house, and the Sun and the lord of his house, and the lord of the hour and the Part of Fortune <note>A mathematical point in the chart calculated from the positions of the Sun, Moon, and Ascendant, representing well-being and success.</note>, and search for the one which will have several authorities and which will be in the best place.** 

And if you do not find any of the things I have told you, look at the lord of the ascendant or the <gloss>lord</gloss> of the **exaltation** <note>A specific sign where a planet is considered to be at its maximum strength and dignity.</note> after him, and the lord of the **term** <term>A division of a zodiac sign (typically 5 to 7 degrees) ruled by a specific planet.</term> and of the **triplicity** <term>A group of three zodiac signs belonging to the same element—fire, earth, air, or water.</term> and of the **face** <term>A ten-degree segment of a zodiac sign, also known as a decan.</term>. Learn which of them is strongest in the ascendant by the multitude of its **dignities** <term>The various ways a planet gains strength or "essential dignity" based on its position in the zodiac.</term> and that it gazes upon the ascendant; and you shall designate that one as the **significator** <note>The planet that represents the person asking the question or the subject of the inquiry.</note> if it is in a good place. And the goodness of the place is that it be in any of its dignities or in a good place regarding its distance from the Sun or in the **angles** <term>The most powerful houses in a chart: the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th.</term> and free from evils or **malefics** <note>original: "infortunes". Specifically the planets Saturn and Mars, which were traditionally believed to cause delays or harm.</note>. Therefore, search for the one who will be strongest and have several dignities and be in the best position. **And know that when the lord of the ascendant is in the ascendant, he will be most worthy in that position.**

### Example of an Interrogation.

An interrogation took place in which the **ascendant** was the 10th degree of **Taurus**. And **Venus**, the lady of that sign, was in the 14th degree of **Cancer**—that is to say, in the house of the **Moon**. But she was joined to Venus by a **quartile aspect** <note>original: "quart regard"; an aspect of 90 degrees, usually indicating tension or a challenge.</note>, and she was **received** <term>'Reception' occurs when a planet is in a sign governed by another planet that it is also in aspect with, showing mutual support.</term>. And the lord of the **Part of Fortune** <term>A calculated point in a chart representing prosperity and well-being.</term> was **Saturn**, who was peregrine in the sign of **Leo** and was not received at all.

And so, looking at all the planets among themselves, I found none stronger in its place than Venus. For she was the lady of the ascendant and was also in her triplicity. And if the Moon had joined with Saturn or Mars, I would have said that she would die after as much time.

¶ Finally, look into the intention and into changeable things and into all things of which you may be asked; that is to say, that in this manner you may know what end the matter will have, and similarly how quickly the time of the effect of things will arrive according to this example. And mix the significations of the planets with the significations of the signs. And thus the matter of every intention will appear to you.

## To find the significator:

### Chapter on the knowledge of the significators of intention
drawn from various sayings of the ancients. Know that the **intention** <note>original: "lentencion"; in this context, it refers to the specific hidden question or motive the seeker has in mind.</note> is more refined when the seeker has held it and thought about it in his heart for a day and a night or more; for if he does not know how to ask about his intention, you will not be able to find the significator of the seeker's intention according to the **thought** <note>original: "cogitacion"; the mental deliberation of the seeker before posing the question.</note> of his heart. Therefore, if someone asks you well, you will not err at all, if God wills; but not every man knows how to ask well. And now I will tell you something about intention so that if the seeker asks you properly, you will not err, if God wills. And what I have found most powerful regarding the significators is that you establish the first significator as the **lord** <term>the planet that "rules" a specific zodiac sign</term> of the **ascendant** <term>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of the question</term> and the planet that receives the **disposition** <term>the "management" or influence of one planet over another based on their positions in the zodiac</term> of that lord. For the intention will be according to the place of the lord of the ascendant in the circle [the zodiac], or according to the place of the receiver of its disposition in the circle—that is to say, that from these places is taken the significance of that very intention. Also, the intention is found from that same degree of the ascendant and from what you observe regarding which planet is joined to the ascendant degree, for the intention will be according to the place of that same planet in the circle. And do not neglect the planet that is in the ascendant, unless it is removed from the ascendant degree, for the intention or thought will be according to the substance of that same planet. Therefore, look at which **house** <term>one of the twelve divisions of the astrological chart, each representing a different area of life like money, health, or travel</term> of the circle the lord of the ascendant belongs to, for the intention will be according to...

### Chapter on the concealment of a ring 
### or of another thing, as it shall be found according to Dorotheus.

<image-desc>A large decorative initial "C" at the beginning of the section. When you have so concealed and hidden a **ring** <note>original: "anel"</note> or any other thing in some place in the house, and you wish to find it, establish the ascendant as accurately and as well as you can; afterwards, divide the house <note>Here, "house" refers to the physical building or room where the object is lost, rather than an astrological house.</note> into 4 parts, of which one is eastern, the other western, the other southern, and the other northern. Afterward, look to where the **lord** <term>the planet that rules the zodiac sign</term> of the ascendant is located. For if it is in the eastern line, the hidden thing is in the eastern quarter of the house; then divide that same quarter into 4 parts as I did before with the other three parts. Afterward, look at the lord of the aforementioned eastern sign in which you found the lord of the ascendant. And if the lord of that eastern sign is in a northern sign, take the northern quarter of the last division, and divide it into 4 as before, and look where the lord of that northern sign is. For if it is in the western line, take the western quarter of the last division and do as with the others, dividing that quarter as before; and if the lord of that western sign is in a southern sign, take the southern quarter of the last division...

# The chapter on how to find, by means of the quadrant, the thing...
# **T**his is the figure for finding hidden things

occult <note>original: "occultes"; meaning hidden or secret</note> according to the Rule of the **Hyleg** <term>original: "hyleus"; the "giver of life," a planet or point in a birth chart used by ancient astrologers to determine longevity and vital force</term>—that is to say, the significator of life or the place of life. By this rule and by the quadrant, and by the pleasure of God, hidden things are found, provided the practitioner knows well how to orient the **ascendant** <term>the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the specific moment of the inquiry</term> and knows how many degrees each planet has passed in the sign where it is. But if he does not know how to find the sign, nor knows how many degrees it has passed in the sign where it is, nor knows the specific part of the sign, he will find the general area <note>original: "la partie"</note> but he will not find the specific location. This is the Rule: you shall look at the ascendant and its lord <note>the planet that rules the zodiac sign appearing on the ascendant</note> and the lord of the hour <note>the planet governing the specific planetary hour of the day</note>, and see which of them "looks" at <note>original: "regarde"; refers to an "aspect" or geometrical relationship between planetary positions</term> the ascendant best. And establish that one as the **significator** <term>the planet chosen to represent the object or person in question</term> and look at where it is located. And if it is in the ascendant, the hidden thing being sought will be in the middle of the East—that is to say, if it is at the degree of the ascendant, that thing will be on the eastern line of the quadrant. And if it is in the 10th [house], it will be toward the South <note>original: "midi"</note> to the right when you look East. And if it is in the 4th [house], it will be toward the North <note>original: "septentrion"</note> to the left when you look East. And if it is in the 7th [house], it will be behind you when you look East. And if you find the significator between the ascendant and the 4th house, it will be between East and North. And if it is in the second house from the ascendant, it will be closer to the East than to the North.

**Of another approach when one searches for hidden things**

When one asks about treasure or any hidden thing <note>original: "chose occulte"</note>, look at the seventh sign from the ascendant sign; if you find fortunes <term>benefic planets, usually Jupiter or Venus, which bring good luck</term> there, know that in that very place there is some hidden thing. Likewise, look in the angle of the earth <term>the Imum Coeli or the 4th house, representing the lowest point of the sky and the ground</term>, that is to say the fourth sign from the ascendant; if you find any fortune there, know that there is something buried. And if it is in a house and you want to know in which part of the house it is, look at the sun and the moon; and if they look at the ascendant, the thing will be in the middle of the house. And if the lord of the hour is in the ascendant, it will be above the door of the house. And if [it is] in the middle of the sky <term>the Midheaven or 10th house</term>, it will be in the middle of the house. And if in the 7th house, the thing will be in the eastern part of the house. And if in the 4th, it will be toward the north <note>original: "septentrion"</note>. And if the lord of the hour is oriental <term>rising before the sun, indicating something in the early stages of its journey</term>, the thing will have been buried recently; and if he is occidental <term>setting after the sun</term>, it will have been buried and covered for a long time <note>original: "de pieça"</note>. Look also at Mercury, and if he is in the ascendant, the thing will be buried in the earth; and if Venus is in the ascendant, it will be under the bed; and if Jupiter is in the ascendant, it [will be] under the wall <note>original: "paroi"</note>. And if Saturn is in the ascendant, it will be in a stinking and dark place.

## Chapter on hidden things according to Ptolemy

This **significator** <term>the planet that represents a specific person or topic in a chart</term> that Ptolemy speaks of is not simply the ruler of the **Ascendant** <term>the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, nor the Moon, nor the planet that holds the most dignity in the Ascendant alone. Instead, it is the one to whom several "fortitudes" <note>astrological strengths or dignities</note> are joined; that is to say, the one who has dignity in the Ascendant and in the places of the **luminaries** <term>the Sun and the Moon</term> and is not "joined" to any other. This means that he does not "commit his disposition" <note>a technical term where a planet passes its influence to another via an aspect</note> to another, and that he is strong in his own place. Or, perhaps, he is joined to another, and that other is joined to no one and is strong in his own place.

Such a planet will be the **significator** of the intention and of the **thought** <note>original: "cogitacion"; in horary astrology, this refers to the specific unspoken question on the seeker's mind</note>. And the place where he is located will signify that intention or thought; that is to say, if he is in the "royal house" <note>the 10th house</note> the intention will concern a king. And if he is in the "house of pilgrimage" <note>the 9th house</note> it will concern a pilgrimage, and so on for the other houses.

And for the assessment of the strength of this significator, there are several other authorities; that is to say, that he should have dignity in the degree of the **conjunction** or the **opposition** <term>the New Moon or Full Moon</term> that occurred before the inquiry, and that he should have dignity in the Ascendant of that same conjunction or opposition, for then he will be stronger. # In the name of God begins the "Little Messahallah" <note>Māshā’allāh ibn Atharī (c. 740–815) was a Persian Jewish astrologer whose works were foundational to European astrology</note> concerning the matters of eclipses of the moon and the sun, and the **conjunctions** <term>the meeting of two planets at the same degree of the zodiac</term> of the planets, and the **revolutions of the years** <term>charts cast for the moment the Sun enters Aries, used to predict the year's events</term> briefly explained. And it contains 12 chapters.

The first chapter is on the nature of the celestial sky and the stars and how they surround this world.

¶ The third is on the **signs** <term>the twelve signs of the zodiac</term> and their natures—namely hot and cold—and also those which are **movable** <term>the cardinal signs (Aries, Cancer, Libra, Capricorn) which mark the beginning of seasons</term>, **diurnal** <term>active by day</term>, or **nocturnal** <term>active by night</term>.
¶ The fourth is on the revolution of the years.
¶ The fifth is on the eclipse of the moon and its significance.
¶ The sixth is on the **mutation** <term>change or shift</term> of the seasons by the movement <note>original: "mutation"</note> of the planets passing through the signs.
¶ The seventh is on the eclipse of the sun and its significance.
¶ The eighth is on the conjunction of the planets and their operations.
¶ The ninth is on the conjunction of the higher planets <note>the outer planets known at the time: Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars</note>.
¶ The tenth is on...

### The first chapter: On the nature of the celestial heaven and of the stars, as follows:

Māshā’allāh <note>original: "messehala"; an influential 8th-century Persian Jewish astrologer and astronomer</note> says that the Most High Lord, who sent him into this world, made the earth in the likeness of a sphere and made the highest heaven rotatable in a circle and according to its movement. And He placed in the middle of the circle the earth, fixed and immovable, deviating neither to the right nor to the left; and He placed the four elements <term>earth, water, air, and fire</term> as movable things—that is to say, He made them to be movable through the movement of the seven planets.

¶ And the Head of the Dragon <term>the North Node of the Moon, the point where the Moon's orbit crosses the ecliptic</term> and the [zodiac] signs and all the stars participate with the seven planets in their natural works.

¶ Therefore, the work of the planets in this world is similar to the magnet and the iron. For just as the iron is attracted to that stone across a great and known distance <note>original: "longitude"; here referring to the range or reach of the magnetic force</note>, so every creature and all things that are upon the earth are made by the movement of the planets; and also the good fortune or misfortune of all things upon the earth, as much of seeds as of beasts. And suitability <note>original: "couenablete"; meaning harmony or fittingness</note> or destruction is caused by the movement of the planets and by their works; from which the diversity of men in their being and in their fortunes and misfortunes is of very great significance. For we see some reasonable men who are poor in goods, and some fools who abound in wealth. And this implies no cause suggesting that fortune and misfortune occur without the judgment and free will of those who experience <note>original: "seuffrent"; here meaning to undergo or permit</note> the knowledge of these fortunes and misfortunes. And all this is the work of the planets, and their fortune and misfortune is by the will of God.

### The 2nd chapter: On the division of the earth; that is to say, of the work of the stars in the countries and regions of the earth.

Māshā’allāh <note>original: "messehallach"; an 8th-century Persian Jewish astronomer/astrologer</note> says that between various planetary states, there is variation according to the diversity of climates <term>climates: the seven latitudinal zones of the habitable world in ancient geography</term>; for in some climates certain planets are fortunate and others are wicked, and in another climate there are different arrangements. And for this reason, every rational person ought to understand the nature of climates and regions, such as the region of the Ethiopians <note>medieval term for the inhabitants of sub-Saharan Africa</note> where there is always heat, and the land of the Slavs <note>original: "esclaves"; referring to the people of Eastern Europe/Russia</note> where there is always cold. And if the planets signify excessive cold for the citizens of Ethiopia, their air will be tempered <note>the "cold" omen balances the natural "heat," resulting in mild weather</note>. And if the planets had signified this <insert>cold</insert> to the Slavs, their air would be corrupted and their dwelling place would grow worse.

And for this reason, the science of the reasoning of climates and regions and years is necessary. And know that the science of the stars is very exalted. And the most worthy thing that can be known from it is that which is universal and general, such as the revolutions of the years and of eclipses, and understanding the significations of all these things. And this is not accomplished except through the knowledge of the natures of the [zodiac] signs and what each of them signifies regarding regions and provinces and all things. And concerning what has been told to us before in our books, let us now briefly have a portion of those things and begin with the help of God.

### Of the eclipse of the moon and of its significance.

**Māshā’allāh** <note>original: "messehallath"</note> **says:** It behooves you to carefully consider the universal eclipse, of the moon as well as the sun, and to know the ascendant <term>ascendant: the degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment</term> of the mid-eclipse. And to know that planet which will have lordship over the said ascendant; and in the figure <note>the astrological chart</note> of that moment, if that planet has been malefic, it signifies hindrance. And if it has been fortunate, it signifies perfection <note>a successful or favorable outcome</note>.
¶ And know that when the eclipse of the moon is in cold signs, it will signify a force of cold. And if in water signs, it signifies a multitude of rains—at least if the season ought to have them, that is to say, if it is winter. And if it [the season] should be [warm], it will signify a tempering of the air.

**Chapter 6: On the change of weather by the movement of the planets according to Māshā’allāh.**

**Māshā’allāh says: When you wish to know** the variation of the weather and the movement of the heavy planets <term>heavy planets: the outer planets—Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars—which move more slowly through the zodiac and were thought to have a "weightier" influence on world events</term> over the things of the world that are corruptible <note>subject to decay and change, such as the environment and biological life</note>, know the signs of the heavy planets—namely, whether they are hot or cold. Those which, if they are hot, will signify heat in summer and mildness <note>original: "attemprance"</note> in winter. And if they are in cold signs, they will signify cold in winter and mildness in summer. 

Similarly, when the planets are prominent in water signs, they will signify excessive rains and a corruption of the air in winter, and very great rains, mildness of the air, and a multitude of dew in summer. And according to this, test it and you will not err. 

¶ The heavy planets are Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars, and you must also join the Sun to these. And know also that when the heavy planets have been in the near regions <note>referring to the observer's specific latitude or the northern celestial hemisphere</note>, they will signify dryness, sterility of the earth, famine, and similar things. And when they are distant from them, they will signify a multitude of rains. And I intend that by "proximity" <note>original: "ppiquite" or propinquity</note> it be understood that they are in northern signs, and by "distance" that they are in southern signs. 

Know also that when the heavy planets are all oriental <term>oriental: appearing in the eastern sky before the sun rises</term>, they signify dryness; and when they are occidental <term>occidental: remaining visible in the western sky after the sun sets</term>, they signify a multitude of rains in winter and mildness of the air in summer. And similarly, when they are high above the horizon, they signify a pleasantness of the air in winter and too great a dryness in summer.

# Chapter 1: On the eclipse of the sun and its significance

Māshā’allāh says: Know that an eclipse of the sun cannot occur without signifying a great event, depending on the magnitude of the eclipse—that is to say, when it covers a quarter of the sun or more. And the knowledge of the coming event of the solar eclipse requires you to know the <term>ascendant: the sign of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon</term> at the middle of the eclipse and the planets ruling over the <term>figure: the astrological chart or map of the heavens</term> of the eclipse. If these planets are <term>malefic: "unfortunate" planets, traditionally Saturn and Mars</term>, they signify hindrance, corruption, and the death of kings and the wealthy. And if they are <term>benefic: "fortunate" planets, traditionally Jupiter and Venus</term>, they signify good fortune and celestial harmony in all things.

Know above all that when an eclipse of the sun occurs in the sign of the Ram <note>Aries</note>, it signifies the death of kings and the wealthy, the sterility of the land, or drought and famine—and likewise in the other <term>fire signs: Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius</term>. In <term>water signs: Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces</term>, it signifies a multitude of rains and worsening conditions or hindrances caused by them. 

Understand also that when benefic planets "look upon" the eclipse <note>form an aspect or geometric relationship with it in the sky</note>, they will diminish those hindrances. And when malefic planets look upon it, they will increase the said hindrance and diminish the good fortune. Know also that when the sun or the moon is at any <term>hyleg: the "giver of life," a point in a chart used to determine longevity</term> or <term>alchocoden: the "giver of years," a planet that determines the length of life</term>, if they are obscured [by the eclipse], those for whom they are the hyleg or alchocoden will face great peril or illness, unless benefic planets look upon them.

For the benefic planets signify good fortune and a good outcome if they prevail over the chart of the conjunction. And if the malefic planets prevail there, they will signify an evil outcome of misfortune.

# Māshā’allāh says: **the 10th chapter on the conjunction**

He says: we have already said and recounted before that the greatest things—the most great and principal occurrences—come from the "greatest conjunction," which is of the highest planets. Among others, the conjunction of Saturn and Jupiter is the "very great conjunction," and it signifies <term>accidents: specific historical events, disasters, or changes</term> and <term>sects: the rise and fall of religions or political movements</term>. For the science of these accidents is understood by looking at the <term>ascendant: the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> and the planets at the hour of their conjunction, to determine which of them has <term>lordship: the dominant astrological power or "governance"</term> in the <term>figure: the astrological chart</term>. If that planet is fortunate, it will signify good and favorable weather; and if it is <term>malefic: original "maleus," meaning having a harmful influence like Saturn or Mars</term>, it will signify worsening, drought, sterility of the earth, and wars.

Know also that when Jupiter is stronger than Saturn, it will signify good in that same conjunction. And if Saturn is the victor, it will signify worsening and tribulation in that conjunction. ¶ And know that their conjunction in signs of fire and air signifies drought, sterility of the earth, and the war of hunger <note>meaning famine caused by or leading to conflict</note>. And in signs of earth, it signifies the destruction of seeds and famine. In the lines of water, it will signify an excessively large quantity of rain and pestilences, provided that they are <term>hindered: "empeschiez," meaning their positive influence is blocked by other celestial factors</term>. And if, in the end, they are fortunate, they will signify an increase of good and a decrease of evil in all their significations. (Furthermore, know that when the greatest conjunction shall be

And if its ruler is <term>afflicted: weakened or poorly positioned</term>, this will signify his death and downfall. And one should fear the conjunction after the known years of that conjunction—the fifteen years of which we have made mention elsewhere.

### Chapter 4: On the middle conjunction

Māshā’allāh says: the middle conjunction is the conjunction of Mars and Saturn, and it signifies <term>accidents: specific historical events or disasters</term> of battles, wars, and controversies <insert>¶</insert>. And when you wish to know these events, know the visiting ruler in their conjunction; if it is fortunate, it will signify good and the suitability of the state of things, and if it is evil, it will signify misfortune and hindrance. Know also that their conjunction in <term>human signs: zodiac signs depicted as humans, like Gemini, Virgo, or Aquarius</term> signifies a multitude of illnesses among the people. And their conjunction in any of the <term>angles: the four most powerful points in an astrological chart</term> of the year signifies the poverty of the wealthy and of kings, and the instigation of wars. And their conjunction in fire signs signifies drought and the sterility of the earth. And in air signs, they signify winds. And in water signs, <note>original: "yaue" (eau)</note> a multitude of rains. And in earth signs, frost, snows, and a great deal of cold. And know also that when <term>fortunes: benefic planets like Jupiter or Venus</term> look upon them, they will diminish the evil, and the <term>malefics: harmful planets</term> will increase it.

### Chapter 5: On the lesser conjunction

Māshā’allāh says: the lesser conjunction is the conjunction of Mars and Jupiter, and this signifies the events that occur in rains and clouds, and signifies the corruption of the air and war. And if, in their conjunction, a fortune is the victor, it will signify good fortune, and if [it be afflicted...]

<meta>This page concludes the astrological treatise by Māshā’allāh, specifically finishing the discussion on the conjunctions of planets and their effects on world leaders and social order. It transitions from the previous page’s discussion on the elemental signs (water signs) into general rules for interpreting planetary alignments.</meta>

...evil in equal measure. <note>original: "maluez agal" - likely concluding a thought from the previous page regarding harmful conditions in water signs.</note>

¶ And know that when <term>Jupiter: traditionally the "Great Benefic," representing growth and justice</term> and <term>Mars: the "Lesser Malefic," representing war and energy</term> are <term>conjoined: positioned at the same point in the zodiac</term> in the <term>ascendant: the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the start of the year</term> or within the <term>angles: the four most powerful points in an astrological chart (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses)</term> of that same year, it will signify commotion and <note>original: "diuersite" - meaning conflict, instability, or variety of competing claims</note> upheaval among kings, unless a <term>fortune: a beneficial planet, such as Venus or Jupiter</term> is looking upon them.

Know also that every time a fortune is joined with an evil planet, the influence of whichever is the stronger of them shall prevail. And when an evil planet is joined with another evil planet, then evil will so abound, unless the one who holds <term>lordship: the primary astrological influence or "ruling" power over a specific sign</term> in that conjunction is a fortune.

Understand, this is the final word that we have pronounced in this book.

¶ Finished is the book of <note>Māshā’allāh ibn Atharī (c. 740–815 AD), a leading Persian Jewish astronomer and astrologer whose work was central to the medieval European understanding of the stars.</note> Māshā’allāh, translated by <note>John of Spain (Johannes Hispalensis), a prolific 12th-century translator known for bringing Arabic scientific and philosophical works into the Latin-speaking world.</note> John of Spain.
